Market analysis

OIL FUTURES: Crude dips as US reportedly pushes for renewed talks with Iran
US reportedly contacts Iran to restart diplomacy
Escalation risks hold floor under prices: analysts
Hormuz vessel traffic drops to 16 transits
Crude prices settled lower Oct. 29 amid hopes that renewed calls for US-Iran diplomacy could avert regional escalation, analysts said.
NYMEX December WTI ended the session down 78 cents/barrel at $102.00/b and ICE December Brent declined 44 cents/b to $114.88/b.
Crude prices have oscillated this week amid an ebb and flow of headlines regarding Middle East diplomacy efforts, but near-term escalation risks remained in focus, analysts said. Price turned lower overnight following media reports stating that US Vice President JD Vance had contacted senior Iranian leadership to propose the restart of talks between Washington and Tehran, but escalation concerns held a floor under prices, analysts said.
"[Oil] dropped sharply after reports that Vice President Vance had reached out to Iranian hardliners, briefly encouraging traders to lean toward diplomacy rather than another round of military escalation," said Quintex Intel Strategist Stephen Innes.
NYMEX November RBOB settled 1.16 cents/gallon higher at $3.1850/gal and November ULSD declined 7.92 cents/gal to $4.7360/gal.
"Persistent headlines of deal talks are weighing on the crude oil market. We expect this will continue into the US midterms, but do not anticipate Iran would agree to a deal just before the tense elections," TD Securities analysts Ryan McKay and Bart

International Crude
Dated Brent differential falls $6/b in four sessions as supply balance lengthens
November loaders struggle to clear amid oversupply
End-month cargoes heard at discount to Dated Brent
The Dated Brent physical differential has fallen $6.20/b across four sessions as North Sea November barrels struggled to find homes amid a well-supplied market, exacerbated by a lack of arbitrage opportunities out of Europe due to punitive freight rates.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Dated Brent physical differential at 6 cents/b Oct. 29.
The Dated Brent differential reflects the most competitive differential among Brent, Forties, Oseberg, Ekofisk, Troll and WTI Midland grades each day during the 10-day to one-month forward assessment period.
"Freight has made the Atlantic basin locked, most of it needs to clear locally," a Europe-based North Sea crude trader said, pointing to a long European supply balance for the second half of November.
"It seems there are a few bits and pieces left behind that aren't going to achieve the premiums that got paid for baseload," said a second trader.
Market participants were viewing falling offers over the Oct. 27-29 Platts MOC sessions for November barrels of WTI Midland and North Sea grades on a FOB basis as bearish indicators for the complex as a whole -- particularly with estimates for crossUK Continent freight being above $6/b, said traders.
"It seems people have been influenced by WTI Midland offers in recent days. We've been hearing very bearish [sentiment]," said a third trader, noting that North Sea sellers had been seeing falling bid levels for over-the-counter cargoes in recent sessions.
The Oct. 29 Platts Market on Close assessment process saw US producer Occidental offering an end-November arrival WTI Midland CIF Rotterdam cargo, left unsold at a $4.10/b premium over Dated Brent.
"There is definitely less WTI Midland going East," said a fourth trader, adding that more volumes should be pointing at Europe as a result.
While Asian refiners had recently been seen buying WTI Midland volumes on Aframaxes "as Afras were more economic than VLCCs; compared with Murban, it is still expensive, so

Meanwhile, November-loading North Sea grades were also under pressure, with prompt cargoes still available and exerting pressure on differentials amid the release of December-loading programs over Oct. 28-29.
The Oct. 29 MOC session saw two November-loading Oseberg and one Brent Blend cargo emerge on offer, left outstanding at the London close without finding a home. Traders had also reported unsold availabilities across November-loading Ekofisk and Forties in the over-the-counter market.
As a result, traders had pegged premiums for end-November Forties cargoes as low as negative to Dated Brent during Oct. 29 European afternoon trading, while Ekofisk over the same period was heard as low as $3/b premiums.
In response to the bearish physical picture across all segments, backwardation across the North Sea Dated strip had narrowed sharply over the week, while prompt Brent CFD contracts settling against early-November had flipped into a contango.
The Brent CFD settling across Nov. 9-13 was assessed at a $6.43/b premium over its Dec. 7-11 counterpart at the Oct. 26 London close -- the spread between the two contracts had narrowed to a $1.72/b premium by the Oct. 29 close.
"It's a very volatile market, [with a] very bearish prompt [in the] last couple days, and next week's physical values were being implied negative during [Oct. 28 European morning trading]," said a fifth trader. "December rolls remain strong; it's just early November that is very weak."
-- Ernest Puey, Natasha Tan
PetroChina expects oil prices to stay elevated on geopolitics in Q4: executive
Middle East conflicts drive supply concerns
PetroChina targets 8 million mt shale oil annually
Q3 refined product exports up 27% year over year
International oil prices are expected to remain elevated in the fourth quarter of 2026, potentially edging higher from Q3, a company executive said during PetroChina's Q3 briefing conference call on Oct. 29.
CFO Wang Hua said geopolitics remained a key driver of oil prices, with conflicts in the Middle East increasingly becoming more complicated and prolonged. Disruptions to global oil supplies and historically low inventories were also expected to keep oil prices elevated.
The anticipation came despite Q4 being a traditionally seasonally weak period for oil consumption, with demand expected to decline from the previous quarter, according to Wang. Coordinated releases of strategic oil reserves by several countries and growing expectations of further interest rate hikes by the US Federal Reserve could also put pressure on oil prices, he added.

Dated Brent averaged $96.99/b in Q3, down from $103.85/b in Q2, according to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
In the longer term, Wang said the energy transition is expected to push oil consumption into a plateau. However, concerns over energy security and demand for inventory replenishment would continue to provide support to oil prices.
Against this backdrop, Wang said PetroChina would continue to increase domestic reserves and production, restore output from its Middle East projects and raise production from projects outside the Middle East to ensure stable supplies of lowcost crude.
Vice President Yang Weisheng said the company would continue to step up exploration and development during the 15th Five-Year Plan period (2026-2030), with annual capital expenditure to remain above Yuan 40 billion. Annual shale oil production is targeted to exceed 8 million mt, while the company's total crude oil output would remain above 107 million mt/year, accounting for more than half of China's crude production, Yang said.
In Q3, PetroChina recorded oil and gas equivalent output of 456.2 million barrels, up by 1.3% quarter-over-quarter, according to the company's results.
In the first three quarters, it produced 1.38 billion barrels of oil equivalent of oil and gas, including domestic output of 1.2e billion boe, up by 1.1% year over year, and overseas output of 128.6 million boe, the report showed.
Product exports Wang also said overseas refining margins for oil products
remained relatively high, with gasoil and jet fuel cracks at about $60/b.
The Asia/Singapore jet fuel crack averaged at $64.67/b as of Oct. 29, up from $55.82/b in September and $59.68/b in August, Platts data showed. The monthly average gasoiil cracks in Asia/ Singapore remained hovering at high levels, although retreating to $62.72/b as of Oct. 29 from $63.98/b in September and $67.72/b in August, according to Platts data.
PetroChina will continue to conduct oil product exports in an orderly manner and on market-based principles, in line with government policy and taking into account domestic product supply and sales as well as export economics, Wang said.
Chinese refineries are required to prioritize ensuring domestic supplies rather than the export economy, while they were newly awarded 5.56 million mt quotas for clean oil product exports in the fourth batch quota allocation, Platts reported.
PetroChina exported 13.41 million mt of oil products and bonded bunker fuel in the first nine months of the year, including 5.98 million mt in Q3, up about 27% year over year, according to Wang.
Its crude throughput rose 11.1% quarter over quarter to 346.9 million barrels in Q3, while its refining margin reached about $12/b, up about $3/b from the previous quarter, Wang said.
In January-September, PetroChina processed 1 billion barrels of crude oil, down 3.7% year over year, while refined product

PetroChina's revenue rose 7% year over year to 2.32 trillion yuan in the first three quarters, and net profit attributable to shareholders rose 21.9% year over year to 155.78 billion yuan, according to the report.
-- Oceana Zhou - Oil Market Specialist
Aker BP trumpets project milestones in Q3 despite lower oil output
Production drops to 374,000 boe/d in Q3
Income jumps 50% on elevated energy prices
Arctic Wisting field nearing 'concept select'
Aker BP recorded one of the "most important quarters" in its history in Q3 from a project perspective, with progress on three major oil and gas developments, even as hydrocarbons output fell 40,000 barrels/day year over year, it said Oct. 29, while profits surged on high commodity prices.
Posting its quarterly earnings, the company - which counts service group Aker and UK major BP as its biggest shareholders -

said it had pumped 374,000 barrels of oil equivalent/d of oil and gas in Q3, compared to 384,000 boe/d in Q2 and 414,000 boe/d a year ago.
The drop was mainly due to "planned maintenance," Aker BP said, with Q3 the most active month for turnarounds on the Norwegian Continental Shelf. Aker BP is also a shareholder in Johan Sverdrup, which has seen an 11% year-to-date decline, Equinor said Oct. 28.
Aker BP has retained its full-year production guidance of 380,000-400,000 boe/d, and still hopes to lift output above 500,000 boe/d from 2028 and into the 2030s, it said.
Despite the drop in output, the company struck a bullish tone after delivering "key milestones" on a trio of offshore projects.
It brought the Skarv Stallites project onstream in August, a year ahead of schedule and completed its ninth subsea tieback on the development it said, while the key Yggdrasil and Valhall PWP-Fenris offshore projects are both on track for 2027 startup, following the installation of key infrastructure, it said.
Along with operator Equinor, Aker BP is also progressing toward the 2027 startup of the third phase of the 755,000 b/d Johan Sverdrup field, which contributes a third of Norwegian output.
There will also be four new exploration wells drilled in its Norwegian Continental Shelf portfolio in the fourth quarter, Aker BP said, none of them operated.
"Offshore, this was one of the most important quarters in our project history," CEO Karl Johnny Hersvik said in the statement. "With the Hugin B, Munin and Valhall PWP topsides installed

"As we look beyond the current project portfolio, we see substantial opportunities for continued value creation on the Norwegian Continental Shelf," he added.
"We are strengthening our opportunity set through exploration and new partnerships, while using technology, AI and a more integrated operating model to unlock more resources and improve how we work."
Aker BP recently signed a deal with fellow Norwegian players Equinor and Var Energi to cooperate on stand-alone frontier exploration opportunities on the NCS.
Elevated prices Aker BP's total income in the quarter rose almost 50% year
over year to $3.87 billion, it said, with the US-Iran war sending oil and gas prices soaring following the effective closure of the Strait of Hormuz.
In its update, Aker BP said the income boost - which led to strong profits of almost $1 billion - was "driven by efficient operations, higher sold volumes and strong realized prices."
In recent months, Norwegian producers have sold cargoes into Europe at hefty premiums to Dated Brent, which Platts last assessed at $120.61/b on Oct. 28. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
Aker BP said it achieved a liquids price in the quarter of $102/b, compared to $70/b in Q3 2025. Although that was down slightly quarter over quarter, Aker BP said it had boosted its "volume sold" by 20,000 boe/d in the quarter.
The company's capex guidance for 2026 remains unchanged at around $6.8-$7.2 billion, it said.
However, the company expects capex to ramp up from 2028 thanks to the 500-million-barrel Wisting oil field in the Arctic Barents Sea. The long-delayed project "is approaching concept select," Aker BP said, with FID expected in around a year, according to operator Equinor.

Indonesia, UAE discuss oil supply of potentially up to 30 million barrels
UAE proposes crude delivery to Jakarta: minister
SE Asian country imports 1 million barrels daily
B50 biodiesel to cut foreign oil needs by 25%
Indonesia and the UAE are discussing the potential supply of up to 30 million barrels of oil, Energy and Mineral Resources Minister Bahlil Lahadalia said Oct. 28, noting that the talks were preliminary.
"It is still a possibility. Everything is still at the preliminary stage," Lahadalia said. Details on the delivery period were not provided.
Djoko Siswanto, the chief of state-owned oil and gas regulator

SKK Migas, said at an industry event Oct. 27 that the UAE had raised the possibility of supplying up to 30 million barrels of oil to Indonesia. Siswanto said the proposal was conveyed by the UAE ambassador during a meeting he attended alongside representatives of Mubadala Investment Co. The meeting also covered the final investment decision for a gas project in Andaman, Aceh.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, contacted the UAE embassy in Jakarta for comments Oct. 29, but had yet to receive any confirmation at the time of publishing.
Siswanto said the supply of up to 30 million barrels of crude oil came "without cost" and without requiring working capital, with shipping also to be provided free of charge, state-owned news agency Antara reported Oct. 27. Siswanto did not elaborate on the proposed cooperation mechanism, financing, delivery schedule or precisely which costs would be waived, Antara reported.
Indonesia is seeking to strengthen energy security as the Southeast Asian country is heavily reliant on imported oil and fuels.
Indonesia consumed about 1.6 million barrels/day of fuel, while domestic oil production stood at about 600,000 b/d, leaving about 1 million b/d to be covered by imports, Lahadalia said.
Lahadalia said the planned introduction of B50 biodiesel, comprising a 50% palm oil-based component, could reduce import requirements by about 250,000-300,000 b/d, leaving about 700,000-750,000 b/d to be met through imports.
-- Mia Pei, Anita Nugraha
Europe set to tap 42 mil barrels of oil stocks: French minister
EU ministers coordinate release over three months
France to release 10 mil barrels of diesel 'at cost'
Move follows Iran war, intense US pressure
European countries will release more than 42 million barrels of strategic oil reserves in the next three months, with 10 million barrels of diesel hitting the French market "at cost" in the next few days, France's economy minister, Roland Lescure, said Oct. 29.
Speaking on arrival at a gathering of EU ministers in Luxembourg, Lescure said officials were pushing ahead with "the liberation of strategic reserves of petrol and diesel," following a G7 decision on Oct. 2 to release 100 million barrels of oil in response to soaring fuel prices stemming from the US-Iran war.
"Less than a month later, Europe has coordinated a very important release of strategic reserves," Lescure said. "We are going to release more than 42 million barrels altogether in the next three months, and half of this is going to be diesel."
Diesel cracks hit record highs this year amid constraints on the export of crude and products through the critical Strait of Hormuz. Lescure said diesel was "already at the heart of our challenges." The Oct. 29 meeting was focused on energy security

October 29, 2026

ahead of the more challenging winter months. "France is playing its part fully," he said, noting that Paris
would release 10 million barrels, all of it diesel, into the French market in "the next couple of days," bringing down the price of diesel at the pump.
Crucially, France's diesel release will be done "at cost, not at current market prices," Lescure said. "As soon as next week, there will be a seeable impact on gasoline prices," which should dip by about 12-18 euro cent per liter, he said.
The short-term relief is designed to help consumers in the winter months, Lescure said, with European officials hoping the Middle East crisis can be resolved in medium term.
If not, they are keeping "our options open" for further releases, the French official said. "In the long run we need to stop depending on petrol," he added.
The 100-million-barrel release is being overseen by the International Energy Agency, which represents mostly wealthy energy consumers, and follows intense pressure from the US' Trump administration for European countries to tap their stockpiles.
In March, the IEA announced a historic 400-million-barrel emergency stock release just weeks after the war with Iran began. Its governing board said Oct. 15 that some 330 million barrels had been released under the plan, and members would take steps to complete the remaining releases of approximately 100 million barrels. The release of some 20 million barrels of diesel would be accelerated, it added.
Some members have released more barrels than initially promised, while others intend to do so, meaning the ultimate draw will exceed the original commitment, the IEA has said
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed European ultra-low sulfur diesel on a CIF basis at $1,509.50/metric ton on Oct. 28, slightly below its $1,642.25/mt peak in mid-September.
Dated Brent was assessed at $120.61/b on Oct. 28, following months of extreme volatility since late February.

CRUDE MOC: Middle East sour crude complex sees two convergences
25 Dubai partials trade during MOC
Two Dec-loading Oman crude cargoes declared
Platts Dubai, Oman premiums fall 61 cents/b
The Middle East sour crude complex saw two convergences during the Singapore Platts Market on Close assessment process Oct. 29, while cash differentials for key sour crude markers declined for the fifth straight session.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed December cash Dubai, Oman and Murban at premiums of $5.07/b to same-month Dubai futures at market close, down 61 cents/b day over day.
During the MOC, 25 December Dubai partials, each of 25,000 barrels, were traded. The sellers were Vitol, Mercuria and Unipec, while the buyers were Trafigura and Centalion, formerly known

as Gunvor. Unipec and Vitol each declared a cargo of December Oman
crude to Trafigura following convergences of 20 partials in Platts cash Dubai.
A convergence occurs when 20 partials are traded between two counterparties in the same direction, resulting in the declaration of a full 500,000-barrel physical cargo from the seller to the buyer.

NNPC lowers most of Nigerian crude oil OSPs for November cargoes
NNPC cuts 34 of 37 crude grades for November
Usan drops $13.46/b, largest decline overall
Bonny Light falls $4.79 to 2-cent discount
Nigerian National Petroleum Co. lowered most of its crude oil official selling prices, also known as NSPs, for November-loading cargoes, according to a copy of the pricing document seen by Platts on Oct. 28.
Of the 37 crude oil grades listed in the document, 34 declined from their October levels. Obe rose by 37 cents/barrel to a $1.07/b premium to benchmark Dated Brent, while Okwuibome Condensate finished up 8 cents/b to a $2.20/b premium.
Usan fell the most across the full suite of Nigerian grades, with its NSP set at a $13.13/b discount, down $13.46 month over month.
Flagship grade Bonny Light saw its NSP fall by $4.79 to a 2-cent/b discount, while Forcados, which is regularly Nigeria's largest export grade, saw its NSP set at a $1.34/b premium.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Alexander Saucer
THAILAND DATA: Sept crude, condensate imports fall 17% YOY to 873,969 b/d
UAE leads as Thailand's top crude supplier
Domestic crude production jumps 25.5% on year
Asian condensate refining economics weaken
Thailand imported 873,969 barrels/day of crude oil and condensate in September, down 17.1% year over year, according to Customs Department data released Oct. 26.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

6

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

Crude imports accounted for the bulk of the total at 855,310 b/d, down 16.1% year over year and 19.9% from August, with the UAE being the largest supplier.
The September decline came as broader Asian seaborne sweet crude imports fell 25% month over month to 5.6 million b/d, with Russia, the US and Brazil as the main suppliers, according to S&P Global Energy CERA's latest crude market outlook. Purchasing remained sensitive to freight and grade-specific economics amid competition from sour crude, the outlook said.
Despite the sharp monthly decline, Thailand's crude imports remained broadly steady on a year-to-date basis. The country imported 967,851 b/d of crude during January-September, up 0.7% year on year, with supplies mainly from the UAE, the US and Saudi Arabia, customs data showed.
The country's condensate imports contracted more sharply, with September arrivals down 44.8% year over year and 12.7% month over month to 18,659 b/d, and Saudi Arabia as the main supplier.
Over January-September, condensate imports averaged 27,582 b/d, down 20.8% year over year, with the US as the largest supplier.
The weaker condensate imports came as Asian condensate refining economics softened in September. S&P Global Energy said condensate refining values weakened during the month as softer petrochemical demand pressured naphtha cracks, although it expected values to strengthen in subsequent months on firmer gasoline margins, year-end restocking and seasonal winter demand.
Across crude and condensate, Thailand's imports averaged 995,433 b/d in the first nine months of 2026, little changed from the same period of 2025.
Domestic production rises Thailand's domestic crude production averaged 100,434 b/d
in January-August, up 25.5% year over year, according to Energy Policy and Planning Office (EPPO) data released Oct. 12.
Sirikit remained the largest crude-producing field at 23,133 b/d, down 1.4% year over year, EPPO data showed. Tantawan production rose 36.6% to 18,679 b/d, while Erawan output surged 168.7% to 15,904 b/d, accoding to the data.
Condensate production averaged 75,849 b/d over the same period, down 2.4% year over year. Bongkot produced 22,334 b/d, down 1.1%, while Erawan output fell 19.2% to 20,209 b/d and Arthit production rose 12.8% to 16,910 b/d, data showed.
CERA's October outlook expects Thailand's segregated condensate production to continue on a general downward trajectory.
Thailand has about 1.24 million b/d of refining capacity in 2026. Refineries including Thai Oil, IRPC, PTT Global Chemical and Star Petroleum Refining processed 1.067 million b/d of crude and condensate during January-August, down 0.9% year over year, according to EPPO data.
Bangchak's 114,000 b/d Phra Khanong refinery was operating at full capacity in late September despite severe flooding in Bangkok, the company told Platts Sept. 28. The plant is the only

major Thai refinery within Bangkok, with most of the country's other refining capacity concentrated around Sri Racha and Map Ta Phut/Rayong.
In the condensate market, PTT was heard in September to have purchased two November-loading Muda condensate cargoes from Petco, scheduled for Nov. 1-7 and Nov. 23-29, respectively. Petco was also heard to have sold PTT a Cakerawala condensate cargo for loading Nov. 1-10, according to several trade sources.

CHINA DATA: Refiners eye Nov run cuts as crude costs tighten margins
At least five refiners plan Nov run cuts
Sinopec throughput may fall about 5% MOM
Tight product supplies support PetroChina runs
Crude throughput at China's state-owned refineries faces further downward pressure in November as high feedstock costs and weakening margins prompt some plants to consider voluntary run cuts on top of scheduled maintenance, refinery sources told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, Oct. 27-29.
At least five state refiners under Sinopec Group said they planned to lower utilization rates in November to limit refining losses, while PetroChina Company Limited's throughput is expected to be supported by relatively tight domestic gasoline and gasoil supplies and the return of capacity from maintenance.
A Sinopec refinery in South China plans to cut its utilization rate to about 80% in November, from about 93% in October, as high freight and insurance costs worsen processing economics.
Sinopec could cut crude throughput by up to 5% to about 4 million barrels/day in November, according to sources familiar with the matter.
About 820,000 b/d of state-owned refining capacity, mainly at Sinopec plants, will be under scheduled maintenance in November, up from 744,000 b/d in October, Platts data showed.
Utilization rates at Sinochem and China National Offshore Oil Corporation are expected to remain largely stable, while maintenance at PetroChina Guangdong Petrochemical Co Ltd is not scheduled to begin until early December.
Sinopec refiners weigh deeper cuts "All refiners want to cut throughputs, but the obligation to
secure oil product supply in the domestic market has determined the operating rate," a South China refinery source said.
The refinery expects to operate at about 70%-75% of capacity to meet its domestic supply obligations.
Chinese refiners have been facing widespread processing losses amid relatively high crude prices, elevated freight costs and steep crude differentials, while domestic oil product price adjustments have lagged increases in feedstock costs, according to another South China refinery source.
"It would not be surprising to see state-owned refiners voluntarily cut runs under the current economics," the source said.
"Margins are deeply negative. Luckily, we will be undergoing maintenance next month," a state-owned refinery source in East China said. "With freight and insurance costs remaining high, refiners have little incentive to raise runs."
The pressure could intensify in December, as another stateowned refinery source in East China said, "Run rates will probably have to fall further in December as there won't be enough crude available."

State refinery throughput eases in Oct The combined crude throughput of 49 Chinese state-owned
refineries was expected to fall to 8.15 million b/d in October, from 8.39 million b/d in September, Platts data showed.
Throughput stood at 8.13 million b/d in August, 7.77 million b/d in July and 7.75 million b/d in June.
The 49 refineries, with a combined capacity of 10.7 million b/d, comprise the majority of Sinopec and PetroChina's refineries, as well as CNOOC's Huizhou Petrochemical and Sinochem's Quanzhou Petrochemical.
October throughput translates into an average utilization rate of 76.1%, down from a six-month high of 76.4% in September.
Tight supply supports PetroChina's runs PetroChina faces less pressure to cut November runs, with
company sources pointing to relatively tight domestic gasoline and gasoil supplies.
China's gasoil inventories fell about 4% month over month to 10.78 million mt as of Oct. 23, the lowest since Jan. 6, 2023, local information provider JLC data showed.
"From this point of view, refiners should not cut throughputs," a PetroChina refinery source in North China said.
Refiners that rely more heavily on imported crude face greater operational pressure because China's domestic gasoline and gasoil pricing mechanism does not fully reflect changes in crude costs, making it more difficult to pass higher feedstock costs through to consumers, the source said.
PetroChina's average refinery utilization rate recovered to about 84.7% in October from about 82% in September.
Its 200,000 b/d North China Petrochemical refinery, Huabei Petrochemical, is expected to return to typical operations in the second half of October following scheduled maintenance, supporting PetroChina's overall crude throughput in November.
PetroChina's Dalian Petrochemical is also preparing to restart units associated with its 200,000 b/d crude distillation unit, which is expected to come online toward the end of November, refinery sources said.
Independent refiners also cut runs Weak refining margins have also driven smaller independent
refiners in eastern Shandong province to cut utilization toward the end of October, according to JLC. Their weekly utilization rate fell to 50.7% as of Oct. 21 from 53.3% Sept. 30.
Theoretical refining margins deteriorated to a loss of 436 yuan/metric ton as of Oct. 21 from a profit of 694 yuan/mt at the end of September, according to JLC.
"The run rate has been falling the whole month and is likely to fall further in November, maybe slightly below 50% to about 49%," a JLC analyst said.
China's private mega-refiners also trimmed crude runs in October amid higher feedstock costs.
Zhejiang Petroleum & Chemical, Hengli Petrochemical (Dalian), Yulong Petrochemical and Shenghong Petrochemical were expected to process a combined 1.5 million b/d of crude in October, down from 1.52 million b/d in September, the data showed.

North Sea BFOET crude loadings set at 496,774 b/d for Dec
Total of 22 cargoes to load, one fewer than November
Ekofisk accounts for largest proportion of loadings

BFOET crude loadings in the Platts Dated Brent basket are expected to average 513,333 barrels/day in November, down from 536,667 b/d scheduled for November, according to loading program data compiled by Platts.
A total of 15.4 million barrels is due to be loaded in December across 22 cargoes, down from 16.1 million barrels currently scheduled for November.
The November loading program was initially 16.8 million barrels before a Troll cargo, associated with parcel number T1103, was deferred into December.
One Brent Blend cargo is scheduled to load in December, unchanged month over month.
Forties and Ekofisk cargoes are scheduled to load seven and eight cargoes, respectively, in December, both down by one cargo from November.
Three Oseberg cargoes are scheduled to load in December, unchanged from November's program.
Troll is scheduled to load three cargoes in December, up from two in November.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Dated Brent at $120.605/b on Oct. 28.
Ekofisk is expected to account for the largest share of BFOET volumes in December at 36%, followed by Forties at 32%, Troll and Oseberg at 14% each, and Brent Blend at 5%.

Platts Asia Pacific Sweet Crude Daily Market Analysis
Favorable naphtha economics weigh on light condensates
Chinese end-users heard to have procured January-delivery barrels of Tupi crude
Offers for Canadian crude to China heard for delivery from Dec-Jan
The Asia-Pacific sweet crude market saw increasingly bearish sentiment on Oct. 29, possessing both the condensate and crude complex, according to several crude traders.
On the condensate segment, December-loading barrels of North West Shelf condensate barrels saw pressure from much more competitive arbitrage condensate barrels, coupled with diminished buying interest from end-users who prefer to procure naphtha instead of condensates as a splitter feedstock.
"Arbitrage offers on landed are way lower than [the offers for December-loading barrels of North West Shelf condensate cargoes.] ME sellers still enjoy under this high FP environment," a Singapore-based trader said.
"Naphtha economics are now better because of stronger LPG content ... so it needs to be compared to naphtha values to make sense for splitters to choose this [North West Shelf condensate] grade over naphtha," the trader said, attributing that generally a cargo of NWSC yields about 75% naphtha.
Elsewhere, a South Korean end-user was heard to have procured 1 cargo of Saudi Arabia's Jafurah condensate, for loading in November, marking its second purchase of the grade after having taken one which was delivered to South Korea in early October, various trade sources said.
Over on the medium-sweet crude front, another Asia-based trader added that premiums of key Malaysian crude barrels could be further depressed if freight rates were to ease.
"Only freight is making it more attractive compared to further [arbitrage] crude barrels, but if freight continues to come off it will be tough market," the trader said.
Over on the arbitrage crude front, January-delivery barrels of Brazil's Tupi crude were heard to range at a premium of $10/barrel to Platts December Dated Brent crude assessments, DES China, a trade source said.
Elsewhere, offers for Canada's Access Western Blend crude - set to be delivered over December to January - were heard at a premium in $5/b to ICE Brent crude futures, DES China, according to another market participant.
In statistical news, Thailand imported 873,969 barrels/day of crude oil and condensate in September, down 17.1% year over year, according to Customs Department data released Oct. 26.

On the upstream crude oil futures front, prices slipped as global markets digested the US Federal Reserve's rate decision amid continued US-Iran dialogue.
Despite ongoing dialogue between US and Iranian representatives, the market remained pessimistic about the prospects for a longer-term peace deal, analysts said.
"We continue to assess the risk of further military escalation after the midterm elections as significant," Arne Lohmann Rasmussen, chief analyst at GRM, wrote in an Oct. 29 note.
Meanwhile, the US Federal Reserve held interest rates steady Oct. 28, in line with market expectations.

Platts Middle East Sour Crude Daily Market Analysis
Platts Dubai differential falls for fifth day
Chinese refiners eye November run cuts
Middle East crude differentials continued to weaken Oct. 29 as spot activity wound down toward the end of the month.
The Platts Dubai cash-futures spread fell for a fifth day, down 61 cents/barrel at a $5.07/b premium at the Oct. 29 Asian close.
Crude throughput at China's state-owned refineries faces further downward pressure in November as high feedstock costs and weakening margins prompt some plants to consider voluntary run cuts on top of planned maintenance, refinery sources told Platts over Oct. 27-29.
At least five state refiners under Sinopec Group said they plan to lower utilization rates in November to limit refining losses, while PetroChina Co. Ltd.'s throughput is expected to be supported by relatively tight domestic gasoline and gasoil supplies and the return of capacity from maintenance.
Meanwhile, China's independent refineries are increasing purchases of Iraqi crude as Iranian barrels vanish from the market, refiners, traders, analysts and bankers told Platts on Oct. 28. However, the Iraqi volume is unlikely to fully compensate for

the reduction in Iranian crude for the sector. In recent news, Indonesia and the UAE are discussing the
potential supply of up to 30 million barrels of oil, Energy and Mineral Resources Minister Bahlil Lahadalia said Oct. 28, adding that the talks were preliminary.

Platts Mediterranean & Black Sea Sweet Crude Daily Commentary
CPC still under pressure, Azeri Light rises
European refiners report strong Q3 margins
Mediterranean sweet crude differentials remained under pressure Oct. 29 as ample supply in the region and limited arbitrage opportunities continued to weigh on the complex.
Even though lower CPC Blend differentials could make the arbitrage into Northwest Europe viable, there was still volume available in the region, while the Asian arbitrage remained closed.
"CPC has to compete with WTI Midland in the North, and land maybe $3-$4/b cheaper," one Mediterranean source said.
The Kazakh grade was particularly weak in the prompt, with some upside seen for December, but this was capped by rising Atlantic Basin supply, particularly into Rotterdam.
In North Africa, Algeria's Saharan Blend continued to trade at a discount to its official selling prices as it tried to compete with the same supply weighing on CPC. Libya's Es Sider remained firmly under Dated Brent, capped by Azerbaijan's Azeri Light, which has seen an uptick in buying appetite amid good diesel yield and freight economics which are less punitive for short-haul barrels.
"Diesel-rich grades like WAF and North Sea are landing much more expensive [than Azeri], so the move on Azeri is not as quick as what we see on other comparable barrels," another Mediterranean source said, adding that upside remained limited for now.
In industry news, European refiners highlighted strong refining margins in their third-quarter results, Platts reported Oct. 29.
Austria's OMV raised its 2026 European refining margin forecast to $25/b from $20/b after margins averaged $37.50/b in Q3. CEO Emma Delaney said she had not seen margins as high in 30 years in the industry. For 2026, OMV expects the margin for its European refineries to be around $25/b, up from $20/b in its previous forecast and $10.10/b in 2025.
TotalEnergies reported a European refining margin of $33.20/b, more than double Q2 levels, as crude throughput rose nearly 10% on the quarter to 1.55 million b/d. The company expects Q4 throughput to remain broadly stable despite planned maintenance at Normandy. The French energy group's global refinery utilization rate increased to 88% in Q3, up from 80% in Q2 and 84% in Q3 2025.

Platts North Sea Crude Daily Market Analysis
Falling North Sea offer levels keep sentiment bearish as buyers pull back
November availabilities heard unsold across majority of basket grades
North Sea crudes continued on a downward trajectory Oct. 29, as falling offer levels across the complex kept pressure on unsold November loaders amid the fresh release of December loading programs.
"It seems there are a few bits and pieces left behind that aren't going to achieve the premiums that got paid for baseload," said a Europe-based North Sea crude trader.
Market participants continued to view falling offers over previous Platts Market on Close assessment process sessions for November barrels of WTI Midland and North Sea grades on an FOB basis as bearish indicators for the complex as a whole -- particularly with estimates for cross-UK Continent freight being above $6/b, said traders.
"It seems people have been influenced by WTI Midland offers in recent days. We've been hearing very bearish [sentiment]," said a second trader, noting that North Sea sellers had been seeing falling bid levels for over-the-counter cargoes in recent sessions.
The MOC saw US producer Occidental offering an endNovember arrival WTI Midland CIF Rotterdam cargo, left unsold at a $4.10/b premium over Dated Brent.
"There is definitely less WTI Midland going east," said a third trader, adding that more volumes should be pointing at Europe as a result.
While Asian refiners had recently been seen buying WTI Midland volumes on Aframaxes "as Afras were more economic than VLCCs, compared with Murban it is still expensive so Asia will just buy the baseload Midland [volumes]," added the third trader.
Meanwhile, November-loading North Sea grades were also under pressure, with prompt cargoes still available amid the release of December-loading programs over Oct. 28-29.
The Oct. 29 MOC session saw two November-loading Oseberg and one Brent Blend cargo emerge on offer, left outstanding at the London close without finding a home. Traders had also reported unsold availabilities across November-loading Ekofisk and Forties in the over-the-counter market.
As a result, traders had pegged premiums for end-November Forties cargoes as low as negative to Dated Brent during Oct. 29 European afternoon trading, while Ekofisk over the same period was heard as low as $3/b premiums.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Natasha Tan
Platts West African Crude Daily Commentary
Crude participants monitoring relative WAF competitiveness
NNPC lowers most crude official selling prices for November

West African crude trading was subdued Oct. 29, with activity limited as the market moved between the November- and December-loading cycles, while participants continued to assess the competitiveness of regional grades against alternative Atlantic Basin supply.
December lifting schedules had largely circulated, giving participants greater visibility over upcoming supply, although this had yet to translate into significant spot trading activity.
Attention remained focused on the relative competitiveness of West African grades, with traders seeing limited scope for free-on-board cash differentials to strengthen significantly, otherwise risking becoming less competitive against shorterhaul Mediterranean and North Sea alternatives, market participants said.
Signs of softer pricing in the Brazilian market added to that competitive pressure. Brazil's Buzios crude for December arrival in Rotterdam was heard Oct. 29 at a discount to the December average of Platts Dated Brent assessments, after indications at high single-digit premiums in recent weeks.
Activity in the WAF market had been limited to purchases from Chinese independent refineries, also known as "teapots," with one Europe-based WAF trader saying it was a signal that pricing in the region was soft. Djeno crude from the Republic of the Congo had yet to see any of its December-loading cargoes trade, the trader added.
Nigerian National Petroleum Co. lowered most of its crude oil official selling prices, also known as NSPs, for November-loading cargoes, according to a copy of the pricing document seen by Platts late Oct. 28.
Usan fell the most across the full suite of Nigerian grades, with its NSP set at a $13.13/barrel discount, down $13.46/b month over month.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- George Delaney

Patterson-UTI says drilling recovery is tightening US fracking capacity
US fracking capacity faces supply bottleneck
Diesel equipment retirements accelerate
Completion pricing recovers in Q3
US hydraulic fracturing capacity could become a bottleneck as rising drilling activity increases demand for well completions, while equipment retirements, lengthy manufacturing lead times and restrained investment limit the industry's response, Patterson-UTI Energy Inc. executives said Oct. 29.
"The completion demand is going to increase, but it's not clear yet where the supply is going to come from," CEO Andy Hendricks said on the company's third-quarter earnings call.
The US shale industry has added more than 50 drilling rigs since activity bottomed in the spring, but completion demand has yet to fully reflect those additions, Hendricks said. That demand is expected to rise over the next several quarters, just as higher diesel costs accelerate the retirement of older pumping equipment.
Patterson-UTI plans to keep its overall completion capacity relatively steady in 2027, replacing older diesel equipment with natural gas-powered assets rather than expanding its fleet. Hendricks said pricing and returns must improve further before additional capacity becomes attractive, with stronger contracts and visibility beyond a single year also needed to support investment.
Gas-powered equipment tightens Legacy diesel equipment represents around 20% of active US
completion horsepower, according to Patterson-UTI's estimates shared during the call. Higher diesel prices are making some of

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

12

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

those assets increasingly uneconomic to maintain while widening the fuel-cost advantage of equipment capable of running on natural gas.
But replacement equipment cannot be added quickly, Hendricks said. Lead times for direct-drive systems now exceed a year because their manufacturing lines also supply equipment for behind-the-meter power generation, he said. Nearly 90% of Patterson-UTI's active horsepower is expected to run at least partly on natural gas by year-end.
Completion pricing rose by a mid-single-digit percentage sequentially in the third quarter, with most of the increase reflecting higher rates for comparable equipment rather than fleet upgrades. Even so, the company has recovered only about one-third of the approximately 30% pricing decline experienced over the preceding several years through the spring.
Hendricks said Patterson-UTI could recover all of that lost pricing, and potentially more, during 2027, depending on supply and demand with the completion market already somewhat short of equipment, and producers increasingly calling to secure available capacity.
Completion services generated third-quarter revenue of $808 million and adjusted gross profit of $133 million, according to CFO Andy Smith. Pricing gains lifted results despite downtime caused by South Texas sand-mine constraints and Permian Basin trucking shortages. Those issues had eased in October, with the company expecting fourth-quarter completion adjusted gross profit of about $140 million, supported by further pricing gains.
Drilling demand broadens into 2027 Private producers led the initial US drilling recovery, but larger
public operators are increasingly discussing and contracting additional rigs for 2027, Hendricks said. Patterson-UTI expects relatively steady fourth-quarter industry activity before another increase as customer budgets reset, with oil-directed drilling offering the clearer growth outlook and gas-directed activity remaining less certain.
The company averaged 101 operating US rigs in the third quarter and expects about 104 in the fourth quarter, exiting above that level, Hendricks said. Average day-rates have risen from the low $30,000s earlier in the year to the mid-$30,000s, with structurally upgraded rigs earning several thousand dollars more than the company average.
Patterson-UTI has about eight to 10 additional rigs that are economic to reactivate at current pricing. Beyond those, higher activation and upgrade costs would require stronger rates. International opportunities, including potential deployments to Argentina, are also competing for available rigs.
Saudi slowdown weighs on products outlook The stronger US outlook contrasts with near-term pressure
in the Middle East, where lower anticipated drilling activity, particularly in Saudi Arabia, is expected to drive a 5%-10% sequential decline in fourth-quarter drilling-products adjusted gross profit.

The segment posted third-quarter revenue of $110 million and adjusted gross profit of $51 million, its highest quarterly results since the 2023 acquisition of Ulterra, according to Hendricks.
International revenue increased sequentially despite conflictrelated logistics, safety and personnel costs in the Middle East, the segment's largest international market.
Hendricks declined to confirm an analyst's suggestion that insufficient Saudi frac capacity was behind the drilling slowdown. Patterson-UTI has no excess US completion capacity to send overseas, he said.

Whitecap's unconventional division includes its condensateand liquids-rich assets, with 4,700 drilling locations and a 1.5 million-acre land position in the Duvernay and Montney plays in the WCSB, while its conventional assets are located in Alberta and neighboring Saskatchewan and include a 3 million-acre land position and 5,800 drilling sites, according to information on the company website.
"Our forecast for 2027 will continue the operational momentum achieved to date in 2026 and build on the recent results we've managed to achieve on our asset base," Fagerheim said.

In the unconventional division, production is expected to average about 265,000 boe/d next year, with a focus on its facilities at Lator, Gold Creek, Karr, and Kaybob, the release said.

Brazil's Petrobras boosts oil, natural gas output to record high in Q3 on FPSO expansion
Jumps to 3.512 million boe/d
Ramp up of Buzios, Mero FPSOs
Offsets Campos Basin declines
Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras registered record oil and natural gas production for a third consecutive quarter, offsetting declines at mature deep-water fields in the Campos Basin by ramping up output onboard recently installed floating production units and adding seven new production wells in the third quarter, according to the company's latest production and sales report.
Petrobras pumped a record 3.512 million b/d of oil equivalent in the third quarter, up 11.7% from 3.144 boe/d in the same quarter of 2025, the company said Oct. 28 after markets closed. Thirdquarter output also rose 5.3% from the previous quarterly record of 3.336 million boe/d set in the second quarter.
The soaring output represented the latest advance at the Buzios and Mero subsalt production-sharing fields, where Petrobras and its partners have installed a series of new floating production, storage and offloading vessels (FPSOs) since October 2024. The ramp-up of the FPSOs P-78 and P-79, which pumped first oil from Buzios on Dec. 31 and May 1, respectively, played a key role in third quarter production growth, the company said.
The FPSOs P-78 and P-79 both have installed capacity to pump 180,000 b/d, according to Petrobras.
In addition, Petrobras continued to expand production capacity onboard existing FPSOs. The equipment upgrades and modernization projects have allowed Petrobras to squeeze an additional 180,000 b/d from existing FPSOs, or the equivalent of a new floating production unit, according to the company.
The FPSO Alexandre de Gusmao installed at the Mero field was among the latest, reaching 190,000 b/d in September. That surpassed the 180,000 b/d installed capacity of the FPSO, which pumped first oil in May 2025. The FPSO Sepetiba, also installed at Mero, produced 212,000 b/d in September, topping installed capacity of 180,000 b/d.
The FPSO Almirante Tamandare remained Petrobras's top production installation, setting a monthly record of 263,000 b/d in July, the company said. The FPSO's output capacity was 225,000 b/d when it pumped first oil in February 2025.
Petrobras also connected six new production wells in the third quarter, down from 10 new production wells in the first and second quarters, the company said. That included six production wells in the Santos Basin and a single production well in the Campos Basin. Petrobras also lost fewer barrels to maintenance shutdowns in the third quarter.
Total oil production in Brazil in the third quarter was 2.832 million b/d, up 12.4% year over year, Petrobras said. Third-quarter oil output also rose 5.3% from the second quarter.
Subsalt fields represented a majority of oil output, producing 2.454 million b/d in the third quarter, up 15.9% year over year,

Legacy fields decline Output from Petrobras' legacy fields in shallow waters,

Oil output from deep-water fields above the salt layer fell 6.8% in the third quarter to 341,000 b/d, Petrobras said. That was down from 366,000 b/d in the same quarter of 2025. Third quarter post-salt production also declined 4.2% from the second quarter.
Petrobras received 36,000 b/d from onshore and shallowwater production fields, as well as 28,000 b/d from international operations, the company said. Both were in line with recent quarters.
Natural gas production also continued to rise in the third quarter amid increased exports from subsalt fields, especially in the Santos Basin, where the Route 3 gas export pipeline started commercial operations in late 2024. The pipeline connects to a new gas-treatment plant at the Energias Boaventura complex outside Rio de Janeiro that reached full processing capacity in 2025.
Gas output in the third quarter surged 9.6% year over year to 651,000 boe/d, up from 594,000 boe/d in the year-ago quarter, Petrobras said. Third-quarter gas production also jumped 5.2% from the second quarter.

Argentina's YPF to ramp up oil production in 2027 to above target: CEO
End-2026 oil output target already reached
End-2027 output to be surprising
Growth comes with new projects in Vaca Muerta
YPF, Argentina's biggest oil producer, plans to ramp up oil and natural gas production next year, as it deploys more drilling rigs and expands its activity in the Vaca Muerta shale play, CEO Horacio Marín said Oct. 29.
"We are accelerating," he said on a conference call with investors.
Marín, who doubles as the company's chairman, declined to announce the production targets, saying that this and its 2027 spending budget still has to be approved by the board of directors.
"You will be surprised about our production in December 2027," he said.

The state-backed company's current oil production target is to reach an average of 215,000 b/d in 2026 with an exit amount of 250,000 b/d for that year, and then increase this to averages of 290,000 b/d in 2027 and 470,000 b/d in 2030.
YPF is on track to achieve the oil targets for this year, with output averaging 224,000 b/d in the third quarter and now at 245,000 b/d, according to a company presentation.
Marín said production has increased since the presentation was made, saying it's now at a record high of 250,300 b/d.
Speeding up growth This output growth coupled with high oil prices has put YPF "in
a very good financial position to speed up next year," Marín said. YPF has been selling non-core assets, including all of its
conventional oil fields, to focus on Vaca Muerta. So far this year, the divestments have earned it $2.2 billion, helping to boost its cash on hand to $3.7 billion at the end of the third quarter of this year from $2.5 billion at the end of the second quarter. Its net leverage ratio has declined 62% over the past year to 0.81 times from 2.1 times, a sign of its capacity to raise more financing for growth.
"We are in a very good financial position to speed up next year," Marín said.
Facilities bottlenecks He said this means there is no bottleneck in terms of
financing for its expansion, but there is in the lack of facilities in parts of Vaca Muerta.
There is enough takeaway capacity, he said, but not in the processing facilities in the new fields it is developing in Vaca Muerta as it expands to the north and the south of the play from its core hub in the center.
"We are going as fast as we can," Marín said. Looking ahead, YPF is leading or involved in projects to ramp up oil and gas production in the north and south hubs with a total investment of $70 billion over the next 15 years or so. These projects will produce a combined 510,000 barrels/day of oil and 38 million cubic meters/d of gas between YPF and its partners.

Imaz said, while 2026 production is expected in line with its 560,000-570,000 boe/d guidance.

Production from the North America region was 219,000 boe/d in Q3, up from 181,000 boe/d in Q3 2025, due to increased production from Leon-Castile in the US Gulf, as well as unconventionals Eagle Ford and Marcellus, according to the company.

Repsol was also positive on development in Petroquiriquire and Carabobo in Venezuela, with a productive participation contract expected to be in place by year-end. It is in line for about 70,000 boe/d should the greenfield project enter production.
The company said that three of five crude cargoes from Venezuela, which it receives in payment for gas supply, have been monetized, with the two others committed. It has used the cash coming from Venezuela to invest in the country and increase production, Imaz said.
Margin contraction In its downstream business, Imaz said the company's refining
margin could contract in 2027, with freight costs notably biting into costs in October.
Imaz said the company is "working under the basis of" a $19/ barrel margin for 2027 and a premium "probably above $4/b."
Inventory rebuild following stock releases and deferred maintenance would support the 2027 margin Imaz said.
For full year 2026, the refining indicator is expected to average $22/b, up from a nine-month margin of $20.60/b but narrowing from a record Q3 figure of $36.20/b, primarily driven by larger differentials for middle distillates and gasoline, as well as a wider spread between heavy and light crude oils but partially offset by

the negative impact of a time lag in kerosene pricing, according to the company.
Margins contracted during October as a result of rising freight costs, which have seen an "exceptional increase to around $30/b from a previous level of around $2/b," Imaz told analysts. Repsol uses the spot market for about two-thirds of its maritime transport, mostly crude imports, since most of its refinery output is transported internally to the market.
The refining margin for October is seen turning out about $18$19/b, leading to a Q4 average of $22-$23/b with a $8/b premium, he said.

Japan PM unveils gasoline subsidy changes from December, utility bill support
Govt to raise gasoline target price by ¥5/month
Electricity subsidy to reach ¥4.5/kWh in 2027
Subsidy fund balance falls to ¥560B
Japan's Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi announced Oct. 29 that the government will begin raising its gasoline target price of about ¥170/liter ($1.08/liter) by ¥5/l every month from December through early spring, according to the Cabinet Public Affairs Office.
Takaichi said the change is "based on the current record-high subsidy of over ¥50/l and an accumulated fund expenditure of ¥9.6 trillion, including ¥1.4 trillion since the Takaichi cabinet started."
Takaichi said the government will subsidize electricity bills by ¥1/kilowatt-hour in December and ¥4.5/kWh both in January and February 2027, totaling about ¥7,000 in subsidies, including a gas subsidy, for standard households from December 2026 to February 2027. The government will decide on the electricity and gas subsidies for March 2027 based on weather conditions or energy prices.
Japan reinstated its gasoline subsidy policy in March 2026 to curb rising oil product prices following a surge in crude oil prices amid heightened tensions in the Middle East. Under the program, the government fully subsidizes the portion of the gasoline price above its target price of ¥170/l, according to the Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry. The current subsidy for oil products, excluding jet fuel, is ¥52.4/l for the week of Oct. 29-Nov. 4.
The national average retail price for regular gasoline was ¥170.1/l as of Oct. 26, according to the Oil Information Center of the Institute of Energy Economics, Japan.
About ¥180 billion was spent from the gasoline subsidy fund in August, and the fund's balance was around ¥560 billion at the end of September, according to METI.
"We do not make a prediction on how long the government raises the target price or when the subsidy policy ends, though we will take incessant steps to tackle inflation," Takaichi said.

India extends 11 billion-liter ethanol tender deadline to Nov. 5
Grain distilleries face higher supply burden
Corn incentive drops to 2.79 rupees/liter
India's state-run oil marketing companies have extended the bidding deadline for their 11 billion-liter ethanol tender to Nov. 5 from Oct. 29, giving suppliers additional time to submit offers as producers seek revised allocation with changes to feedstock composition and price allocation.
Bids under the first-cycle tender for Ethanol Supply Year 202627 can now be submitted until 5 pm India time Nov. 5, according to multiple local media reports on Oct. 29. Bid opening and evaluation will begin at 5:01 pm on the same day.
The tender covers the ethanol supply year (NovemberOctober), meaning the revised bidding deadline falls after the new supply year has begun.
The oil companies did not provide a reason for the extension, according to the reports.
Grain carries burden The tender excludes offers for ethanol produced from
sugarcane juice, sugar, sugar syrup and B-heavy molasses following the government's decision to reduce sugar diversion to zero during ESY 2026-27.
Eligible routes include corn, damaged food grains, surplus rice supplied by the Food Corporation of India, C-heavy molasses and other feedstocks permitted under the National Policy on Biofuels.
The feedstock restrictions place greater responsibility on grain-based distilleries to meet India's E20 blending requirement.
The tender reduced the corn-based ethanol incentive to 2.79 rupees/liter from 5.79 rupees/liter, lowering the effective procurement price to 68.86 rupees/liter from 71.86 rupees/liter.
The All India Distillers' Association has asked the government

to raise the price to 75 rupees/liter, saying corn prices have climbed to around 26-29 rupees/kg from 18-19 rupees/kg a year earlier.
AIDA has also sought additional FCI rice allocations and availability beyond the first three quarters of the supply year.
The tender permits up to 7.2 million mt of surplus FCI rice, equivalent to 3.24 billion liters of ethanol. Allocations are capped at 1.08 billion liters during each of the first three quarters, with no FCI rice offers permitted for August-October 2027.
Quarterly demand The OMC requirement comprises 3 billion liters for November
2026-January 2027, another 3 billion liters for February-April, and 2.5 billion liters in each of the final two quarters.
Allocations will generally proceed from the lowest ex-millcost feedstock category after preference is given to eligible cooperative sugar mills and dedicated ethanol plants with valid long-term offtake agreements.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed anhydrous ethanol shipment one month ahead (M1), down $16/mt at $742/mt CFR India Oct. 27, for the November shipment.
Platts assessed anhydrous ethanol shipment two months ahead (M2), down $14/mt to $736/mt CFR India, Oct. 27, for the December shipment.

Platts European Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
ARA inventories surge 22.77% to 868,000 mt
FOB ARA Eurobob barges fall $28.75 to $1,042/mt
Export demand stays weak amid high freight costs
European gasoline markets slipped further Oct. 29 as Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp inventories rebounded sharply from a multiyear low, adding to signs of improved prompt availability and limited export demand.
Gasoline inventories in the ARA hub rose 22.77% week over week to 868,000 metric tons in the week ended Oct. 29.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed FOB ARA Eurobob gasoline barges down $28.75/mt at $1,042/mt, while the equivalent cash differential narrowed $8/mt to $6/mt, it's lowest since March 30.
Market participants pointed to healthy refinery runs and sufficient gasoline availability in Europe, with cargoes continuing to move despite weaker cracks and market structure. One market source said there was no real scarcity of gasoline, while Mediterranean supply was broadly balanced.
Export opportunities remained limited. A second market source saw limited near-term pull from the US amid stockbuilding there, while high freight costs continued to challenge the economics of moving European barrels between regional and export markets.
High freight and blending-component costs were also complicating gasoline economics. Market sources pointed to expensive MTBE and butane, with finished gasoline currently more economically attractive than blending.
Despite the softer gasoline market, wider refinery economics remained resilient, supported by stronger distillate margins. Market participants pointed to diesel rather than gasoline as the stronger contributor to refinery margins not fully "collapsing," as one source said.
Platts assessed the front-month gasoline-naphtha spread at $91.75/mt, down $7.75/mt.
European gasoline swaps fell sharply, approaching the monthly roll, as the rebound in ARA gasoline inventories added to bearish sentiment.
Backwardation narrowed across the EBOB forward curve, while Med/North spreads strengthened modestly amid weakness in Northwest European gasoline.

Platts assessed the front-month FOB ARA Eurobob barge swap at $1,036/mt, down $20.75/mt, with the equivalent crack down 35 cents/barrel at $14.35/b.
The November/December spread fell $3.25/mt to $31.25/mt, while the December/January spread fell 50 cents/mt to $17/mt.
The November Med/North gasoline differential, the spread between the November FOB Mediterranean swap and the equivalent FOB ARA Eurobob barge swap, rose $1.50/mt to a $25/ mt Mediterranean premium, while the December spread rose 50 cents/mt to a $24/mt premium.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Friedrich Gutsche
Jet
Singapore jet M1/M2 swaps time spread shifts to contango on China export quota
First contango since June 2025
China releases 5.65 mil mt in fourth export quota batch
Warm Japan winter forecast tempers demand outlook
The Platts FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene M1/M2 derivatives time spread moved to contango for the first time in more than a year, following the release of China's fourth batch of clean oil product export quotas.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at minus 17 cents/b at the 0830 GMT Oct. 29 Asian close, down from plus 86 cents/b the previous session.
The front-month market structure was last seen in contango on June 2, 2025, and was last lower at minus 18 cents/b on Oct. 3, 2024, according to Platts data.
China allocated a rare fourth tranche of clean oil product export quotas, likely consisting of 5.65 million metric tons. This would lift the 2026 total by 1.9% year over year to 42.06 million mt.
In 2025, Beijing allocated 41.41 million mt of quotas for gasoline, gasoil and jet fuel exports, taking into account a 640,000-mt quota swap from fuel oil in early November.
S&P Global Energy CERA analysts estimated that China would need to export at least 2 million barrels/day of clean oil products over the final two months of the year to fully utilize the quotas.
However, they added that oil product inventories were too low to support such export levels.
Nonetheless, the release of additional export quotas has likely helped to snuff out lingering concerns about tightening Asian jet fuel/kerosene balances amid the upcoming peak winter season.
Prior to the release of the fourth batch, market participants attributed uncertainty surrounding whether China's remaining quotas would suffice for the rest of the year as a source of bullishness for the complex.

GS Caltex sells 300,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for Nov. 1214 loading
Pertamina seeks 200,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for Nov. 28-30 delivery
The Asian jet fuel/kerosene complex weakened Oct. 29 amid reports that China had issued a fourth batch of export quotas.
Platts assessed the front-month November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at plus 86 cents/barrel at the Oct. 28 Asian close, down from plus $1.63/b on Oct. 27.
In midmorning Asian trading on Oct. 29, brokers pegged the spread at plus 60 cents/b.
China's release of additional export quotas is expected to ease supply tightness in the fourth quarter, though details of the allocation could not be immediately ascertained.
Meanwhile, seasonal winter demand for kerosene was likely to keep a floor under the complex, although sales remained slow despite inquiries from Japanese buyers, market sources said.
"There is interest, but very little actual money is being thrown around. Not many have actually purchased, and there are some being done cheaply," a South Korea-based jet fuel/kerosene trader said, adding that they were not yet maximizing kerosene exports.
"I think Japanese demand remains firm. Not sure about the real domestic consumption situation in Japan, and this winter is expected to be relatively warm, but there are still several people asking me about kerosene exports," a second South Korea-based

trade source said. South Korean refiners are still awaiting clarity on whether
the country will ease export restrictions imposed in March, which capped kerosene, gasoil and gasoline outflows at 2025 levels. President Lee Jae-myung said on X on Oct. 21 that the government was "preparing to reasonably reduce the mandatory domestic supply quota and allow refiners to expand their export volumes."
In tenders, South Korea's GS Caltex sold 300,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for loading over Nov. 12-14 at a discount of about $1.30/b to the whole-month November average of Mean of Platts Singapore jet fuel/kerosene assessments, FOB Yeosu.
Elsewhere, Indonesia's Pertamina Patra Niaga sought 200,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for delivery over Nov. 28-30 to Jakarta (SKH) via a tender closing Oct. 29, with same-day validity.
The company previously bought 200,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for Nov. 24-26 delivery at a premium of about $7-$8/b to the Oct. 25-Nov. 24 average of MOPS jet fuel/kerosene assessments, CFR Jakarta (SKH).
In data news, India's aviation turbine fuel production rose 14.2% month over month and 5.1% year over year to an eightmonth high of 1.31 million metric tons in September, Petroleum Planning and Analysis Cell data showed late Oct. 28. ATF output was last higher at 1.34 million mt in January, according to PPAC data.
However, total ATF production over January-September remained 11.64% lower year over year at 11.1 million mt.
India's ATF exports rose 30.68% month over month but fell 23.59% year over year to 526,191 mt (138,564 barrels/day) in September. Exports over January-September fell 30.13% year over year to 3.95 million mt.

Diesel
US diesel exports hit 31-week low amid closed arbitrage, rising freight costs
Caribbean imports surge to highest level since 2016
Freight rates climb to six-month peak
US diesel exports have fallen to their lowest level in more than seven months, as closed arbitrage opportunities and surging freight rates weighed on shipments abroad, according to the latest government and market data.

The US Energy Information Administration said Oct. 28 that US ultra low sulfur diesel exports were down by 59,000 barrels/ day to 1.32 million b/d in the week ended Oct. 23. This marks the lowest weekly export volume since the week ended March 20, a 31-week low, highlighting recent weakness in the export market as US barrels lose competitiveness overseas.
A key driver behind the slowdown has been the closure of arbitrage opportunities between the US Gulf Coast and Europe. Platts analysts measured the average USGC-European ULSD arbitrage for October at minus 68 cents/b, reversing sharply from the open $5.29/b average seen just a month prior in September. With the arbitrage window effectively shut, fewer US cargoes have made their way across the Atlantic. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
Data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea showed that US diesel and gasoil exports to Europe in October at 6.8 million barrels, down from 7.4 million barrels in September, as closed arbitrages have reduced the trade flows.
Instead, the Caribbean emerged as the largest discharge region, taking in 16.1 million barrels in October, up by 9 million barrels from the previous month and the highest on record since 2016. East Coast South America was the second-largest destination, with 8 million barrels discharged, led by Brazil's imports of 7.4 million barrels. This is Brazil's highest monthly intake since September 2019, as the country replaces scarce Russian diesel with USGC barrels and builds inventories ahead of refinery maintenance.
Export premiums have also signaled weakening demand. The USGC 62g waterborne export premium fell to 5 cents/gallon over Gulf Coast Pipe, the lowest since March 18. This more than seven-month low underscores the decline in export appetite for US distillate.
Adding to the headwinds, elevated freight costs have made it more expensive to move the refined product. The USGC-UK Continent route for medium range clean tankers was assessed at $111.90/mt Oct. 28, the highest since April 17 and a more than six-month high, further eroding export economics.
-- Carlos Castillo
Power generation boom strains engine supplies as oilfield companies shift away from diesel
Engine orders extend into 2029 and 2030
Caterpillar triples capacity by 2030 target
Oilfield firms wait years for gas equipment
A surge in orders for large industrial engines is testing the manufacturing capacity of companies, as booming demand for electricity generation coincides with an accelerating shift toward natural gas-powered equipment in the oilfield services industry, as global diesel price rally.
The competing demands are creating an environment in which oilfield service companies seeking to replace diesel-powered machinery with more economical natural gas alternatives,

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

20

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

are having to wait in line as manufacturers work to increase production to meet demand from data centers, electricity producers and traditional energy customers.
Caterpillar, one of the world's largest manufacturers of industrial engines, said Oct. 29 that demand for large reciprocating engines and turbines remains exceptionally strong, with orders extending into 2029 and, in some cases, 2030. The company is expanding manufacturing capacity to meet demand, but its ability to increase output remains limited by the pace at which additional production capacity can be brought online.
"Bringing additional capacity online is still the top pacing factor for growth in Power & Energy," CEO Joe Creed said during the company's third-quarter earnings call.
Caterpillar plans to increase large reciprocating engine manufacturing capacity to nearly three times its 2024 level by 2030. The expansion includes repurposing part of its WinstonSalem, North Carolina, facility to assemble and test 3600 Series engines, specifically to support growing demand for natural gas compression.
The company is increasing production, but additional output depends on how quickly new manufacturing capacity can be brought online.
Management said demand remains strong for diesel standby generators and natural gas-fired prime power serving data centers, as well as engines used in gas compression, marine and mining applications.
The expansion in engine demand extends beyond Caterpillar. Finnish manufacturer Wärtsilä reported a 190% year-over-year increase in Energy equipment orders during the third quarter, reflecting demand from data centers, conventional electricity generation and flexible power plants supporting renewable integration.
Wärtsilä expects demand for its Energy business to remain strong over the coming 12 months, although it anticipates market conditions similar to the preceding period rather than further acceleration.
Oilfield equipment deliveries lag For oilfield service companies, elevated diesel prices are
strengthening the financial incentive to replace conventional

hydraulic fracturing equipment with natural gas-powered systems, particularly in producing regions where gas is readily available.
However, obtaining the equipment can take years. Canadian pressure-pumping company Trican Well Service said during its Q3 earnings call that it had received only three of 10 natural gas-powered fracturing pumps ordered approximately a year earlier. New orders for similar equipment may not be delivered until 2028.
Trican said natural gas-powered pumps and associated equipment can displace more than 90% of diesel consumption in certain fracturing operations, generating fuel savings exceeding $100,000 per day for customers.
US oilfield services company ProPetro also highlighted the tight equipment market, saying supplies of powergeneration machinery remain scarce. The company has secured approximately 2.6 GW of generation equipment, including more than 1 GW of Caterpillar 10-MW units, through purchases and long-term supply arrangements.
ProPetro CEO Sam Sledge said access to equipment has become a decisive competitive advantage in power generation. The company also reported that natural gas-powered fracturing fleets are effectively sold out across the industry, with elevated diesel prices sustaining demand for the technology.
The developments highlight the challenge facing oilfield operators: high diesel prices are making natural gas-powered equipment increasingly attractive, but the growing global market for industrial engines is complicating efforts to secure the machinery needed to make the transition.
"You can´t buy what they won't sell you," Trican CEO Brad Fedora said.
