Brazil's Petrobras sets diesel output record in Q3 to meet demand, cut imports
Refineries produce 769,000 b/d Capacity utilization rate at 99% Total Q3 refinery output mixed

Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras produced record volumes of diesel at its refineries in the third quarter to meet domestic demand and reduce the need for imported barrels, although total refinery output and capacity utilization rates were mixed, according to the company's latest production and sales report.
Petrobras-operated refineries produced a record 769,000 b/d of diesel in the third quarter, up 6.7% from 721,000 b/d in the same period of 2025, the company said Oct. 28 after markets closed. Third-quarter diesel production also topped the previous quarterly record of 764,000 b/d set in the second quarter.
Diesel production also set a new monthly record of 792,000 b/d in July, Petrobras said.
The record-setting diesel output came amid the annual peak of Brazil's oil seed and sugarcane harvest seasons, which typically occur in September. Petrobras has increased domestic diesel production to reduce the need for imported barrels, with Brazil typically importing about 25% of its diesel each year. Lower diesel imports would reduce the impact of higher international oil and refined product prices on inflation in Latin America's largest economy.
Petrobras set quarterly ULSD production records at three refineries, including REPLAN in Sao Paulo state, REFAP in Rio Grande do Sul state and REGAP in Minas Gerais state in the third quarter, the company said. REFAP also set a monthly ULSD production record of 59,000 b/d in July.
Total refined production output and refinery capacity utilization rates, however, were mixed in the third quarter, Petrobras said.
Petrobras-operated refineries produced 1.873 million b/d of diesel, gasoline and other refined products in the third quarter of 2026, up 4.6% from 1.79 million b/d in the same period of 2025, the company said. Third-quarter refinery output, however, fell 2.3% from 1.918 million b/d in the second quarter.
Refinery capacity utilization rates were mixed at 99% in the third quarter, up from 94% in the third quarter of 2025 but down from 101% in the second quarter, according to Petrobras.
The quarter-on-quarter downturn was attributed to a planned maintenance shutdown at the RPBC refinery in Sao Paulo state, Petrobras said. Petrobras had previously delayed some of the work to maintain domestic supplies earlier in the year.
Gasoline declines Gasoline production, meanwhile, declined broadly amid
reduced seasonal demand and reduced prices for biofuel competitor hydrous ethanol, the report showed.
Petrobras produced 412,000 b/d of gasoline in the third quarter, down 1.9% from 420,000 b/d in the year-ago quarter, the company said. Third-quarter gasoline production also decreased by 2.8% from 424,000 b/d in the second quarter.
REFAP set a quarterly gasoline production record in the third quarter at 59,000 b/d, as well as a monthly gasoline production record of 62,000 b/d in September, according to Petrobras.
Jet fuel production was also lower amid reduced air travel, the

report showed. Petrobras produced 88,000 b/d of jet fuel in the third quarter, down 1.1% from 89,000 b/d in the year-ago quarter. Third-quarter jet fuel output also fell 19.3% from 109,000 b/d in the second quarter.
Fuel oil and bunker fuel output was mixed in the third quarter, Petrobras said. The company's low-sulfur oil from subsalt fields is ideal for transforming into bunker fuel that meets the latest International Maritime Organization standards.
Petrobras produced 219,000 b/d of bunker fuel and fuel oil in the third quarter, up 7.9% from 203,000 b/d in the same quarter of 2025, the company said. Third-quarter production, however, fell 5.2% from 231,000 b/d in the second quarter.

BRAZIL DATA: Biodiesel output mixed in September, ethanol production broadly lower
Biodiesel falls to 910.3 million liters
Ethanol sinks to 4.08 billion liters
Harvest seasons peak in September
Brazil's biodiesel production was mixed in September, while ethanol output was broadly lower despite the typical thirdquarter peak in the oil seed and sugarcane harvest seasons, according to the latest production report from the National Petroleum Agency, or ANP.
Brazil produced 910.3 million liters of biodiesel in September, up 3.9% from 875.7 million liters in September 2025, the ANP said Oct. 28 after markets closed. September's biodiesel production, however, was down 1.8% from 926.6 million liters in August.
The mixed biodiesel production report followed ongoing government price subsidies and cuts on fuel taxes aimed at mitigating the impact of volatile international oil and refined product prices on Latin America's largest economy. State-led oil company Petrobras also increased diesel output at domestic refineries as well as fuel imports in the third quarter.
Demand for diesel is typically strongest in the third quarter of each year, which is when the oil seed and sugarcane harvest seasons peak. The harvest season starts in March-April, depending on weather conditions, and runs until November. Agricultural demand makes diesel Brazil's top refined product in terms of consumption, according to the ANP.
Brazil also is evaluating higher biofuel-blending mandates to reduce the need for imported barrels of diesel and gasoline. Brazil currently blends 15% biodiesel with diesel sold at the pump, with plans to increase the biodiesel mandate to 16% in 2027, according to the Mines and Energy Ministry. That should help reduce diesel imports by about 2.4 billion liters.
Each 1% increase in the volume of biodiesel blended with diesel sold at the pump also represents about 600 million liters/ year of additional biodiesel production, according to Brazilian Biodiesel and Biokerosene Union, or Ubrabio, and the Brazilian Vegetable Oil Industry Association, or Abiove. Ubrabio and Abiove are both trade groups that represent biodiesel producers.

Ethanol retreats Ethanol production, meanwhile, retreated despite a recent
increase the volume of anhydrous ethanol blended with gasoline sold at the pump, the ANP report showed. Brazil raised the volume of anhydrous ethanol mixed with gasoline to 32%, up from 30%, on Aug. 1, according to the Mines and Energy Ministry.
Brazil produced 4.080 billion liters of ethanol in September, down 15.7% from 4.838 billion liters in September 2025, the ANP said. September's ethanol production also decreased 22.3% from 5.25 billion liters in August.
Ethanol production also usually peaks in September with the sugarcane harvest, which also causes prices for the biofuel to decline. Brazilian motorists typically opt to fill fuel tanks with hydrous ethanol when the price of the biofuel alternative is about 70% of the price of gasoline.
Output of hydrous ethanol, which can be used directly in Brazil's fleet of flex-fuel vehicles, sank 15.1% in September to 2.578 billion liters, compared with 3.035 billion liters in September 2025. September's hydrous ethanol product also dropped 21.6% from 3.287 billion liters in August.
Anhydrous ethanol production also decreased across the board in September, despite the increased blending mandate, the ANP said. Brazil produced 1.501 billion liters of anhydrous ethanol in September, down 16.7% from 1.803 billion liters in September 2025. September's anhydrous ethanol output also tumbled 23.5% from 1.963 billion liters in August.
-- Jeff Fick
South Korea to boost diesel exports amid strong global demand, robust cracks
Seoul raises diesel export cap to 110%
Crack spreads hit record high $83.02/b
Refining capacity 1.1 mil b/d above demand
South Korea will allow refiners to increase diesel exports to alleviate supply shortages in Asian and global markets, as ample domestic supplies give Asia's top middle-distillate supplier greater flexibility to capitalize on robust crack spreads and export margins.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

The South Korean government said it will permit additional diesel exports from November, provided that refiners fulfill domestic supply obligations and maintain adequate inventories, according to an Oct. 29 statement from the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources.
"Export volumes will be allowed at up to 110% of the corresponding month a year earlier, compared with the existing ceiling of 100%," the trade ministry said.
The ministry introduced export restrictions on March 13, alongside a domestic petroleum price cap, to secure local supplies amid concerns over crude import disruptions and heightened geopolitical tensions in the Strait of Hormuz. The restrictions applied to gasoline, diesel and jet fuel/kerosene.
The policy shift reflects stable feedstock crude procurement despite ongoing tanker-flow disruptions in the Strait of Hormuz, while the country's major refiners have maintained relatively high run rates to meet essential fuel demand from domestic and AsiaPacific customers, according to the ministry and feedstock and product managers at three refiners based in Incheon, Ulsan and Seosan who spoke to Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, during market discussions Oct. 29.
South Korea has about 3.4 million barrels/day of refining capacity, well above its domestic petroleum demand of about 2.3 million b/d. The surplus capacity supports its role as a major supplier of transportation fuels and middle distillates across the Asia-Pacific region.
Refiners will need to apply for additional export volumes from this week, and the ministry will assess each company's domestic supply commitments, inventory levels and the market outlook before approving shipments, according to the statement.
"The relaxation would be managed flexibly and could be reversed if domestic supply conditions deteriorate," the ministry said.

from customers and trading companies on the US West Coast and in Indonesia, New Zealand and Europe have also continued, product marketers and sales managers at two refiners based in Ulsan and Seosan told Platts on Oct. 29.
South Korean shipments of refined products to top buyer Australia rose 18.4% year over year to 72.97 million barrels over January-September, while exports to Europe reached 15.44 million barrels over the same period, marking record-high shipments for the first three quarters, the latest data from staterun Korea National Oil Corp. issued Oct. 28 showed.
In spot activity, South Korea's GS Caltex sold 300,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for loading over Nov. 12-14 at a discount of about $1.30/b to the whole-month November average of the Mean of Platts Singapore jet fuel/kerosene assessments, FOB Yeosu, according to three Asia-based trade sources.
The refiner also sold 300,000 barrels of 50 ppm sulfur gasoil for loading over Nov. 21-25 at a discount of around $5-$6/b to the loading-month average of the MOPS 10 parts per million sulfur gasoil assessments, FOB Yeosu, according to three Asia-based market sources.
S-Oil sold three 300,000-barrel cargoes of 10 ppm sulfur gasoil for loading over Nov. 6-10, Nov. 13-17 and Nov. 21-25 at a discount of about $3-$3.50/b to the loading-month average of the MOPS 10 parts per million sulfur gasoil assessments, FOB Onsan, according to three Asia-based trade sources.

Strong demand Demand for South Korean kerosene from Japan has

Inquiries for incremental gasoline, jet fuel and diesel supplies

INDIA DATA: Sept middle distillates exports fall 26% YOY despite higher output
ATF production climbs to 8-month high
Exports rebound on month but slide on year
India's middle distillate production fell 2.77% month over month to 11.05 million metric tons in September, with a decline in gasoil output offsetting jet fuel's increase, data from the Petroleum Planning and Analysis Cell showed late Oct. 28.
On a year-over-year basis, however, total output rose 3.44%, with production of both products rising over the period.
India produced 9.66 million mt (2.4 million b/d) of gasoil in September, down 4.72% month over month but up 3.23% year over year, PPAC data showed. In the first nine months of the year, gasoil output totaled 90.23 million mt, largely unchanged from the same period in 2025.
Meanwhile, co-distillate aviation turbine fuel (ATF) production rose 14.2% month over month and 5.1% year over year to 1.31 million mt in September. ATF output was last higher at 1.34 million mt in January, marking an eight-month high, according to PPAC data.
However, total ATF production over January-September still remained 11.64% lower year over year at 11.1 million mt.

Exports rise MOM, fall YOY India's middle distillate exports rose 12.14% month over month
but fell 26.37% year over year to 2.79 million mt in September 2026, according to PPAC data.
India exported 2.79 million mt (562,977 b/d) of gasoil in September, up 8.54% month over month but down 26.99% year over year. The year-over-year downswing came from a more-than three-year high of 3.37 million mt in September 2025, historical PPAC data showed.
Over January-September, gasoil exports fell 20.14% year over year to 17.38 million mt.
Similarly, ATF exports jumped 30.68% month over month but fell 23.59% year over year to 526,191 mt (138,564 b/d).
Exports in the first nine months of 2026 tumbled 30.13% year over year to 3.95 million mt.
India cut the windfall gains tax on diesel exports and retained the tax for jet fuel at the same level, the country's oil ministry said in a statement released Oct. 16, reflecting tax adjustments for fuel exporters to ensure sufficient domestic fuel supplies amid ongoing global geopolitical tensions.
The new rate of the windfall tax, also known as special additional excise duty (SAED), along with road and infrastructure cess on the export of diesel, will now be 8.5 Indian rupees/ liter, nearly halving from the earlier rate of 16 rupees/liter, the officials said.
The tax on jet fuel was retained at 10.5 rupees/liter for the current fortnight, they said.
-- Shu ling Lee, Mei huey Ng

Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
NWE diesel price drops $43.50/mt
US exports fall 4.28%
European diesel prices fell on Oct. 29, with bearish underlying gasoil futures and in the absence of competitive indications.

Platts assessed the 10ppm CIF NWE flat price down $43.50/ metric ton to $1,466/mt on Oct. 29, driven largely by a sell- off in the front- month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract on the day. The October ICE LSGO contract fell $39.50/mt to $1,436/mt.
The Med diesel cargo flat price similarly weakened, by $42.25/ mt to $1,450.25/mt, alongside futures.
However, diesel cash differentials also declined on the day in the absence of competitive indications in the Platts Market on Close assessment process.
The NWE diesel market is yet to see heightened tightness, despite reduced inflows from the US amid weak transatlantic arbitrage economics, according to one European diesel trader.
Total US diesel exports declined by 4.28% in the week ended Oct. 23, at 1.32 million b/d versus just under 1.38 million b/d the week prior, according to data from the US Energy Information Administration published Oct. 28. The most recent four-week average totaled 1.46 million b/d, down 48,500 b/d, or 3.32%, from the previous four-week period.
The trader said that sustained high flat prices were discouraging end-user consumption, which was destroying demand at the wholesale level and helping to balance fundamentals in the region.
In other news, the European Commission has initiated a dialogue with European refiners to discuss short-term responses to the ongoing disruptions caused by the conflict in the Middle East as well as the medium-term outlook for the sectors' competitiveness, it said in a statement Oct. 28.
"With a challenging geopolitical reality and pressing security needs in Europe, we must ensure reliable supplies for our citizens, businesses and defense," the statement said.
The EC also said following the first meeting held with the refining industry Oct. 28 that "we will work to increase outputs from refining assets in Europe," adding that "the industry has made some suggestions that we need to assess."
The EC noted that after two decades of refinery closures in Europe, which have already reduced domestic capacities by around 10%, divestments have accelerated and "this crisis is a wake-up call."
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sophia Aung

Platts assessed the spread between Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo and FOB Rotterdam 0.5%S barge assessments, or the East-West spread, at $192/mt Oct. 28, widening from $172/mt Oct. 27. Tighter supplies Singapore has faced a shortage of very low sulfur blending
components in recent months. Traders said the closed arbitrage from Europe will also restrict medium sulfur component flows, tightening the marine fuel market further.
A third Singapore-based trader said he expects most November arrivals to be concentrated in the first half of the month, tightening second-half supplies.

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
VLSFO tightness emerges from bunkering demand
Cash differentials rise amid lower ARA stocks
HSFO premiums climb on refinery maintenance
The European fuel oil market was characterized by emerging tightness Oct. 29, driven by improved local bunkering demand and reduced arrivals on arbitrage opportunities.
In the European very low sulfur fuel oil market, participants said improved local bunkering demand and lower stocks in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp region had contributed to some prompt tightness for product.
In the Platts Market on Close assessment process, cash differentials for physical products had been rising this week, highlighting this tightness.
"Local demand is higher than usual," said a Northwest European-focused trader.
While arbitrage volumes to the Americas and Singapore had been constrained by higher freight rates, some vessels continued to leave Europe, further supporting the complex.
"Product is moving on arb but on smaller vessels," said a second trader.
High sulfur fuel oil market participants expected premiums to edge higher, with freight economics and refinery maintenance across the basin tightening prompt availability even as transatlantic cargoes arrived.
The transatlantic arbitrage was open, one trader said, noting that "cargoes are coming over from Canada, Mexico, Venezuela and the USGC."
A second Mediterranean-based trader corroborated this, saying that despite arrivals from the West, "freight is still playing a role for sure."
The market remained tight "with maintenance at refineries across the basin and Latam barrels coming to the Med, which require blending," the trader added.
On the demand side, participants pointed to an improved appetite for the product that had emerged in the Western Mediterranean.
Trading activity in the MOC was quiet Oct. 29, with 2,000 mt traded in the Rotterdam VLSFO barge MOC and 8,000 mt for the HSFO equivalent. There were no trades for fuel oil cargoes.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Joseph Jaffe

In term tenders, Kuwait Petroleum Corp. has concluded negotiations with term buyers for its full-range naphtha contracts for deliveries over December 2026-November 2027, with the contract shifting to a DES basis, several market sources said over Oct. 22-27.
The full-range naphtha contracts were concluded at a premium of $75/mt to MOPJ naphtha assessments, DES Chiba, for the first six months, according to market participants familiar with the negotiations. The differential will be reviewed again later for June deliveries onward, three sources said.
The pricing period will correspond with the same half-month cycle as the delivery period, according to two traders. For instance, a cargo delivered in H1 December would be priced against the H1 December average of MOPJ naphtha assessments.
The volumes negotiated could not be confirmed. Separately, PTT Global Chemical and SCG Chemicals will merge their olefins and polyolefins operations in Thailand into a joint venture, with PTTGC holding a 56% stake and SCG Chemicals 44%, both companies said in separate stock-exchange notices dated Oct. 28. The companies will first restructure their businesses to isolate the assets being contributed. SCG Chemicals' Thai Polyethylene Co. will then amalgamate with a newly established PTTGC subsidiary, which will survive as the joint-venture company, The Siam Cement Public Co., SCG Chemicals' parent, said in its notice to the Stock Exchange of Thailand. The combination will involve no cash consideration. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Petrochemical demand insulates naphtha prices
Propane scarcity boosts steam cracker buying
East-West arbitrage spread widens to $74/mt
The European naphtha market crack strengthened Oct. 29, while the prompt backwardation narrowed.
European naphtha remained supported by strong petrochemical demand and healthy arbitrage opportunities into eastern markets, despite broader weakness across the refined products complex.
"Naphtha in Europe is fairly supported because petrochemical demand is very strong," a trading source said. "And Eastern arbs are healthy."
Petrochemical buying has helped insulate naphtha from recent weakness in gasoline and gasoil, with softer gasoline markets also reducing demand for naphtha from the blending pool.
"So at the moment the petrochemical sector is keeping the naphtha from falling, especially in Europe, but all the other products, like gasoline or gasoil, are all moving down/demand is weakening," the trading source said.
Despite the propane-naphtha spread still being relatively low, steam cracker demand for naphtha has been improving amid constrained propane availability in Europe. With natural gas

prices roofing, refineries are burning LPG for power generation, slashing merchant supply.
"Some refineries are running propane because TTF is very expensive to run for making power," the trading source said. "That leaves some extra small pockets of demand for naphtha into the petrochemical sector."
While petrochemical demand continued to support European naphtha, market participants were monitoring the potential for additional US arbitrage volumes attracted by strong European pricing, weakening demand from the gasoline blending pool following the recent drop in gasoline values, and the prospect of higher Russian naphtha availability if refinery operations remain undisrupted.
Platts assessed the physical naphtha CIF NWE cargo price at $970.25/metric ton, down $14.50/mt from the day before. It assessed the front-month CIF NWE naphtha swap down $13/mt at $944.50/mt, while the crack was up 66 cents/barrel at minus $3.90/b.
Platts assessed the November/December time spread at $42.50/mt, down $1.50/mt on the day, while the December/ January spread was steady at $27/mt.
The front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- was at $74/mt, up $2/mt on the day.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Barbara Fernandez-pita

European butane tightness deepens as refineries retain winter-grade production
Butane prices reach record highs in US market
Refineries keep butane for gasoline blending
Supply shortage spreads across Europe regions
European butane availability tightened through October as refineries retained more production for winter-grade gasoline

29

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

blending, restricting merchant supply while firm US costs and limited international export availability complicated replacement purchases.
Platts assessed butane CIF Northwest Europe large cargo up $269.50/metric ton from Oct. 1 to $1,086.75/mt Oct. 29. The assessment increased $155/mt from Oct. 8, underscoring the sustained strength of the market as seasonal blending requirements competed for available molecules.
The transition to winter-grade gasoline supported butane consumption within refineries, as higher permitted vapor pressure allowed greater incorporation of the product into the gasoline pool. Increased internal use reduced the volumes available for sale, leaving independent blenders competing for a smaller pool of spot supply.
That was much the same in the US, though an early shift to winter-grade gasoline specifications on Sept. 1 pushed up butane gasoline blending demand to strong seasonal levels.
"However, blenders in the New York Harbor and smaller regional markets are facing very thin margins, owing to the high rail and truck delivery costs," S&P Global Energy CERA analysts said in their Oct. 22 US NGLs report.
Prices at record high US Gulf Coast butane prices at Mont Belvieu's Enterprise
terminal were heard during morning trading around $1.995/gal. That's a record high since Platts began assessing the butane price on Oct. 28, 2021.
Due to the thin margins in non-USGC markets, butane at terminals like Conway, Kansas, hold a more than 6-cent premium to butane at Enterprise. A year ago, that premium was just a penny.
Refinery retention consequently supported the market even as elevated butane costs limited its attraction to petrochemical consumers. Weak buying interest from that sector offered limited relief, while gasoline blending continued to absorb available production.
The strength was reflected in butane's relationship with alternative feedstocks. Butane moved from a $47.50/mt discount to naphtha Oct. 1 to a $118.25/mt premium Oct. 28. Its premium over propane widened from $81.25/mt to $239.50/mt over the same period.
The shift highlighted the increasingly strong influence of gasoline blending on butane values, with buyers facing higher acquisition costs both outright and relative to other hydrocarbons.
Similarly, on the USGC, butane's relative strength versus propane has remained over 70 cents/gal. That premium is a record high for Platts, a sign of US butane's particularly strong demand compared with propane.
Tightness extended beyond Northwest Europe. West Mediterranean participants also struggled to secure replacement volumes against limited regional availability and competition for international supplies.
"Market is crazy tight -- impossible to get molecules," one

West Mediterranean market participant said during the month. Limited North Sea availability and lower Middle Eastern
exports constrained the wider replacement pool. European buyers therefore remained dependent on securing imports in a market where stronger demand did not immediately translate into additional accessible supply.
A transatlantic wrinkle Elevated US butane prices further complicated transatlantic
replacement economics. Seasonal demand associated with winter-grade gasoline blending supported US values, increasing acquisition costs at origin even as stronger European prices improved the incentive to ship cargoes across the Atlantic.
This incentive is more evident in US butane loadings year over year to Northwest Europe. Average butane loadings in October were around 26,000 b/d, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea loading data. That compares with just 5,300 b/d on average a year prior.
That trend continues with overall US butane loadings, which is on average at 466,000 b/d for October, versus 351,000 b/d on average a year ago. Much of Asia has looked to the US to fill displaced butane barrels lost with the off-and-on closure at the Strait of Hormuz. The US is loading over 161,000 b/d on average in October to Indonesia, for example, versus 69,000 b/d in October 2025.
Higher Mont Belvieu butane costs weighed on the attraction of propane-butane split cargoes, complicating efforts to source butane alongside propane. The availability and timing of suitable cargoes remained important for buyers seeking prompt relief.
One participant described "high prices considered unsustainable" but expected the strength could persist for a few more weeks before easing.
The prospect of eventual relief offered little immediate reassurance to buyers facing restricted refinery sales and rising seasonal requirements. Additional import arrivals remained an important potential source of supply, while refinery retention continued to limit the availability of locally produced butane.
-- Baran Serdaroglu, Hunter Marrow
European butane cargoes swing to premium over naphtha on winter blending demand
Winter blending demand tightens supply sharply
Gasoline-naphtha spread hits multi-month low
European butane large cargoes moved from a discount to a substantial premium over naphtha through October, as winter gasoline blending demand and increased refinery retention tightened merchant availability, outweighing weaker petrochemical buying interest.
Platts-assessed butane CIF Northwest Europe large cargo stood at $1,103/metric ton Oct. 28, equivalent to 112% of naphtha CIF NWE cargo at $984.75/mt. Butane had opened the month at $817.25/mt, equivalent to 94.5% of naphtha at $864.75/mt.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

30

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

The move represented a $165.75/mt swing in the differential, from a $47.50/mt discount to a $118.25/mt premium. Butane gained $285.75/mt between Oct. 1 and Oct. 28, while naphtha increased $120/mt.
The transition through parity developed early in the month. Butane remained at an $8.75/mt discount Oct. 7, before moving to a $9.25/mt premium Oct. 8. The premium subsequently widened as seasonal blending requirements absorbed supply and replacement availability remained constrained.
Winter-grade gasoline specifications supported greater butane incorporation into the blending pool, strengthening demand within refineries and from independent blenders. Refineries retained more production for internal use, reducing the volumes offered into the merchant market just as seasonal requirements increased.
Higher prices did not immediately draw out additional local supply, while independent blenders competed for molecules that refiners could consume within their own gasoline production.
The shift also marked a change in butane's position within the petrochemical feedstock pool. Its opening discount to naphtha offered a purchase-cost advantage, although actual cracking economics depended on product yields and operating costs. By late October, its outright premium had substantially weakened that attraction, leaving gasoline blending as the stronger source of support.
Reduced petrochemical buying nevertheless provided limited relief while refinery retention continued to restrict merchant availability.
Supply difficulties extended into the West Mediterranean, where buyers also struggled to secure replacement volumes.
"Market is crazy tight--impossible to get molecules," one regional market participant said during the month.
Limited North Sea availability and lower Middle Eastern exports constrained international replacement options. Elevated US butane costs also complicated trans-Atlantic purchases, as seasonal gasoline blending supported demand at origin.
Stronger European values increased the incentive to attract imports, but the cost and timing of replacement cargoes remained obstacles to immediate relief.
One market participant considered high prices unsustainable but expected the strength could persist for a few more weeks before easing.
Butane's move above naphtha, therefore, reflected more than just a rise alongside the wider energy market. The widening premium showed the strength of seasonal gasoline demand in a market where refinery retention had reduced the supply accessible to other buyers.
Naphtha gasoline blending economics sour At the same time, naphtha demand from the blending pool
has been waning, reducing some of the demand pressure that had kept prices so elevated.
Indeed, the gasoline-naphtha spread fell to a multi-month low amid subdued gasoline blending demand, with activity expected

to remain quiet until mid-November. "Gas-nap [was] hammered," a player active in the gasoline
blendstocks market said. "Blending activity seems quiet until mid-November."
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the gasolinenaphtha spread at $99.25/mt on Oct. 28, the lowest since March 31, when it stood at $61/mt.
The narrower spread has weakened the economics of blending naphtha into gasoline, reducing competition for barrels from the gasoline pool and improving availability for petrochemical buyers.
"Reformate prices are low now, so reforming margins are lower too. [We] need cheaper feedstock," the player said.
For much of 2026, strong gasoline margins drew substantial naphtha volumes into the blending pool, while weak cracker economics and cheaper LPG kept petrochemical demand subdued. That balance is now starting to shift as seasonal weakness in gasoline weighs on blending economics and leaves more naphtha available to the petrochemical sector.
"Hearing more blending barrels ending up in the petchem pull," a trading source said.
-- Baran Serdaroglu, Barbara Fernandez-pita
AltaGas eyes FID by end 2026 for Canadian LPG export facility expansion
Expansion to add 60,000 b/d capacity
REEF project work is 90% complete
Sees options for ethane exports to China
AltaGas is targeting a final investment decision by the yearend to add nearly 60,000 barrels/day of new capacity to its LPG export facility at Ridley Island in British Columbia, which is already at an advanced state of construction, CEO Vern Yu said Oct. 29.
"In Prince Rupert, REEF continues to progress with the highest risk work now complete," Yu said on a webcast to discuss the midstream service provider's third-quarter 2026 earnings. "The project is now 90% complete and moving into commissioning. We remain on track for an in-service date in the first quarter of next year."
REEF, or the Ridley Island Energy Export facility, is being built by a partnership between AltaGas and Vopak, with a first-phase capacity of 56,000 b/d of propane and butane for export to global markets, particularly Asia.
AltaGas is also moving ahead with an expansion of REEF - called Optimization 1 - that will add 30,000 b/d, with an in-service date in the second half of 2027, he said.
For Optimization 1, the facility is 40% complete, and the drilling program is now over 50% complete, with the wells expected to be done in early 2027, Yu said.
For Optimization 2, AltaGas has secured key regulatory permits, and final cost estimates are nearing completion, the company said in its earnings release. The company noted that

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 29, 2026

REEF's capital-efficient expansions will further strengthen AltaGas' ability to meet growing Asian demand for North American LPGs.
Q3 LPG exports up 11% Last quarter, AltaGas exported 147,176 b/d of LPG to Asia
across 24 VLGs, up 11% from Q3 of 2025, the release said, noting that the growth was driven by strong global demand for Canadian LPGs.
AltaGas currently has two facilities along the Pacific Coast - the Ridley Island Propane Export Terminal and the Ferndale facility in the state of Washington - for LPG exports.
Last quarter, loadings at RIPET and Ferndale were 89,134 b/d and 58,042 b/d, respectively, the release said. In October, AltaGas took delivery of its fourth contracted VLGC, with two additional time charters scheduled for 2027 and 2029, which will continue to support export growth and maritime risk management.
Meanwhile, LPG export volumes through the Strait of Hormuz remain more than 70% below pre-conflict levels, with less than one ship on average exiting the Strait each day, Yu said.
"Since the disruption began, we estimate that more than 240 million barrels of LPG supplies have been lost. This has resulted in very high Asian LPG prices, with recent pricing approaching peak pricing. As the situation drags on, we've seen a significant increase in long-term export spreads," he said.
For the second half of 2027, export spreads have increased by more than $5/b compared with three months ago. The conflict has also driven spot shipping rates to triple since early 2026, he said.
Disruption in the Middle Eastern supply has highlighted the value of Canadian LPG exports, with global buyers placing a premium on security and reliability, Yu said, adding that AltaGas remains bullish on LPG supply growth in Western Canada.
The planned new LNG developments - primarily led by the second-phase expansion of Shell Canada - and the 1 million-b/d Pacific Link crude oil pipeline to the Canadian West Coast have the potential to add about 280,000 b/d of propane and butane by 2035, he said.
Options for ethane exports For its part, AltaGas is preparing to handle incremental LPG
exports from its REEF facility, Yu said, adding that the dock, the rail loop and utility quarter have been prebuilt to accommodate REEF's ultimate capacity to 500,000 b/d.
"We are also excited about the long-term potential of ethane exports. We have the opportunity to connect the oversupplied Canadian ethane market with strong petrochemical demand in Asia," Yu said.

Platts European Propane Daily Commentary
NW Europe propane supply stays constrained
Refineries switch to propane amid high gas costs
US imports provide relief but demand remains firm
Northwest European propane supply remained constrained Oct. 29, with lower regional production and steady demand expected to keep the market increasingly dependent on US imports through the fourth quarter.
Elevated TTF natural gas prices supported propane consumption as an alternative refinery fuel, adding to demand at a time of limited regional availability.
"Some refineries are running propane because TTF is very expensive," a trader said, referring to its use for power generation.
This fuel demand reinforced a seasonally firm market, with constrained supply supporting physical values despite some relief from incoming cargoes.
"Cash in Europe is decent," the trader said, adding: "I think propane is stronger" for the time of year.

More favorable transatlantic arbitrage economics in recent weeks had encouraged several US-origin vessels toward Europe, easing some pressure on the regional balance.
The additional arrivals helped temper upward pressure on the delivered cargo market, although subdued domestic production continued to support replacement requirements.
Refinery propane consumption left "some extra small pockets of demand for naphtha into the petrochemical sector," highlighting how competing fuel requirements influenced feedstock availability.
The outlook remained dependent on the availability and timing of further imports. With regional production expected to remain subdued and fuel consumption providing additional support, Europe needed to maintain sufficiently attractive import economics to secure winter supplies.
Although increased US arrivals offered near-term relief, steady underlying demand and additional refinery consumption limited the scope for a substantial easing in the fourth-quarter balance.
One bid was observed in the CIF propane cargo market. BASF offered on a TOT23 cargo, CIF Antwerp, for H1 December delivery, at a 100% CIF Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp large December swap plus $30/mt. It was not deemed competitive, and therefore, the assessment was based on trader sentiment.

pool. This strengthened its seasonal outlet as competitiveness against naphtha deteriorated, leaving gasoline demand increasingly important to the regional balance.
Earlier European market discussions had already highlighted the recovery in blending value alongside a reduction in butane's attraction as a cracker feedstock.

KSA says ships cited over Hormuz violations not operated by Sinokor

Sinokor Merchant Marine Co. Ltd. does not directly operate the vessels recently identified by Iran as allegedly violating transit rules in the Strait of Hormuz, the Korea Shipowners' Association said Oct. 29 in a statement.
According to information confirmed by the Korea Shipowners' Association with Sinokor officials, the vessels were not owned by Sinokor, the KSA said.

Sinokor declined to comment directly when approached by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on Oct. 29. A company spokesperson referred Platts to the statement issued by the KSA.
Iran's Persian Gulf Strait Authority had said Oct. 27 in a statement that vessels belonging to the Sinokor shipping company have committed the highest number of passage violations over the past three months, and appropriate enforcement measures have so far been taken against at least seven of them.
"All cargo owners, stakeholders and insurance service providers are hereby warned that as of Nov. 1, legal action will be taken against all vessels of this company," the authority said in a statement on X.
For South Korean shipping and oil companies, the issue is particularly significant because South Korea -- Asia's third largest crude oil buyer -- relies heavily on seaborne imports of various sour crude grades from the Middle East, feedstock managers at major refiners based in Seosan and Ulsan told Platts Oct. 29.

Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
LR rates drop outside Strait of Hormuz as tanker supply rises
Persian Gulf shuttle operations disrupted
MR freight softens across East Asia markets
East of Suez Long Range, or LRs, weakened Oct. 29 with tanker supply expected to increase in the near term, market participants said. Due to the conflict in the Strait of Hormuz, charterers are uncertain about the fate of the "shuttle tankers" bringing out cargoes through the risky waters of the Persian Gulf, brokers said.
The tankers may get stuck in the narrow waterway, or their transit can get inordinately delayed due to the war, which in turn will hurt the itinerary of the ship-to-ship, or STS, transfer in Oman, or West Coast India, they said. Therefore, the charterers are now hiring LRs for STS transfer outside the Persian Gulf at a much shorter, or prompt notice, after they are convinced about the transit of the shuttle tankers, the brokers added. According to market participants, this has added to the market supply.
In a recent fixture, Trafigura's LR1, the Aragon, was placed on subjects at w520 by ATS, for end-November STS Sohar-Japan loading, sources said.
Several brokers said that activity is slow for West Coast India and Oman loadings, with only one outstanding cargo reported, where ATS was seeking an LR1 for loading Nov. 8 on the STS Sohar-Japan route. This cargo will likely be a fresh test on market levels, an LR broker said.
Bearish sentiment is building on LR2s as even lower rate indications are being targeted on key routes, such as for voyages from Oman or West Coast India toward the Far East, though without a firm cargo on the market, the eventual freight is difficult to gauge, a second LR broker said.
An LR1, the Castor I was placed on subjects at $12.35 million by ATC, for loading naphtha Nov. 4 on the Yanbu-Japan route via the Cape of Good Hope, sources said.
Medium Range, or MRs, were sharply lower across East Asia. An MR broker said the softening sentiment in Southeast Asia and ballasters from East Africa easing supply in the region is likely to influence North Asian freight. Rates for cargoes loading in North Asia toward Southeast Asia are expected to remain stable unless

the loading date is prompt, a second MR broker said. The Hafnia Lynx was placed on subjects at w645 by BP for
loading Nov. 8 on the South Korea-Australia route, with an option to discharge in New Zealand, sources said.
Persian Gulf MRs edged higher for loading in West Coast India. With supply availability expected to tighten, rates could inch up, sources said. Some activity was observed within the Strait of Hormuz doing intra-Strait trips, with details not available, a second MR broker said.
The Oaktree was placed on subjects at w560 by Glencore for loading Nov. 9 on the Sikka-East Africa route, sources said.

The East of Suez VLCC market ticked higher Oct. 29, with sources saying tonnage continued to be fixed away from the available list and sentiment remained healthy. However, market participants said a fresh fixture test was still needed to establish where the market currently stands.
"Several vessels in the Middle East are getting covered quietly every day, but charterers are working under the radar, as always. I feel the market will keep firming, but I don't expect a big jump," a VLCC broker said.
For Yanbu loadings via Bab al-Mandab, levels around w740-w750 were heard to have been done, sources said.
"The rate makes sense. If it gets much more expensive, the cargo economics won't work. Earnings are already high enough at these levels, and there are ships willing to do it," another VLCC broker said.
The Suezmax market dropped significantly amid lackluster demand out of the Gulf of Oman, sources said.
"There's no cargo momentum. Everything is going on VLCCs," a Suezmax broker said.

The Southeast Asian Aframax market continued to ease gradually as cargo demand paused after most regional requirements had been covered.

Brokers said Aframax owners would not be willing to compete for the cargoes, given how low LR2 levels had fallen.
The Gulf of Oman and Bashayer markets were also lower, with softening indicative levels.
On fixtures, Chimbusco was heard to have taken a vessel on subjects for the Nov. 10 loading of 80,000 mt of crude from Bashayer to the East.

News
Brazil's Petrobras Q3 refined product sales rise on strong harvest demand
Diesel sales lead increases
Crude oil exports also jump
China top destination at 40%
Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras increased refined product sales in the third quarter amid strong diesel demand from the agriculture sector and subsidies that offset volatile international prices, while record-setting oil output from subsalt production sharing fields pushed crude exports higher.
Petrobras sold 1.854 million barrels/day of refined products such as diesel and gasoline in the third quarter, up 2.8% from 1.804 million b/d in the year-ago quarter, the company said Oct. 28 in its latest production and sales report. Third-quarter sales rose 6.4% from 1.742 million b/d in the second quarter.
The sales growth in the third quarter was attributed to "favorable seasonal factors," Petrobras said. That included growing industrial activity and strong demand from the agriculture sector, where the oilseed and sugarcane harvest

seasons typically peak in the August-September period each year. Petrobras also has increased diesel production in 2026 in an attempt to reduce the need for imported barrels, which usually account for about 25% of demand.
Diesel sales led the gains, Petrobras said. The company sold 849,000 b/d of diesel, up 4.9% year over year and up 14.4% from the second quarter.
In the third quarter, diesel sales also benefited from the government's ongoing subsidy program. Brazil extended the diesel subsidy program and fuel tax cuts for another 30 days in early October.
Gasoline sales, meanwhile, were slightly lower in the third quarter on higher anhydrous ethanol blending requirements, Petrobras said. Brazil raised the volume of anhydrous ethanol sold with gasoline at the pump to 32% from 30% on Aug. 1.
Petrobras sold 401,000 b/d of gasoline in the third quarter, down 0.2% year over year and down 0.7% from the second quarter.
Jet fuel sales, meanwhile, were broadly higher on stronger travel demand during school holidays, Petrobras said. It sold 122,000 b/d of jet fuel in the third quarter, up 3.4% year over year and up 9.9% from the second quarter.
Exports fall, imports rise Petrobras registered mixed net oil and refined product
exports in the third quarter, the company said. Petrobras reduced crude oil exports amid a 12% tax on exports implemented in March, while imports of diesel and gasoline increased on strong domestic demand.
Net exports jumped 27% year over year to 918,000 b/d in the third quarter, up from 723,000 b/d in the same quarter of 2025, Petrobras said. Third quarter net exports, however, sank 14.6% from the second quarter.
Total oil and refined product exports surged 20.7% year over year in the third quarter to 1.251 million b/d, Petrobras said.
Petrobras exported 987,000 b/d of oil in the third quarter, up 21.3% year over year but down 0.9% from the second quarter.
China continued to be the leading destination for Petrobras' crude oil exports in the third quarter, accounting for 40% of oil exports during the period, Petrobras said. Europe was second at 18%, followed by Asia outside China and India at 15%. About 12% of oil exports headed for the US in the third quarter.
Oil and product imports, meanwhile, were broadly higher in the third quarter, Petrobras said. It imported 333,000 b/d in the third quarter, up 6.1% year over year. Q3 imports, however, were more than double the 156,000 b/d imported in the second quarter.
Petrobras imported 129,000 b/d of crude in the third quarter for lubricants production and blending, down 4.1% year over year, it said. Q3 oil imports, however, jumped 44.7% from 89,000 b/d in the second quarter.
Diesel imports also were mixed in the third quarter, falling 8.8% year over year to 110,000 b/d, Petrobras said. Q3 diesel imports, however, were more than five times the 18,000 b/d seen in the second quarter.

European Commission launches dialogue with European refineries
EC seeks to boost European refinery output
Domestic capacity down 10% after slew of closures
Industry urged to increase biofuel production
The European Commission has initiated a dialogue with European refiners to discuss short-term responses to the ongoing disruptions caused by the conflict in the Middle East as well as the medium-term outlook for the sectors' competitiveness, it said in a statement Oct. 28.
"With a challenging geopolitical reality and pressing security needs in Europe, we must ensure reliable supplies for our citizens, businesses and defense," the statement said.
The EC also said following the first meeting held with the refining industry Oct. 28 that "we will work to increase outputs from refining assets in Europe," adding that "the industry has made some suggestions that we need to assess."
The EC noted that after two decades of refinery closures in Europe, which have already reduced domestic capacities by around 10%, divestments have accelerated and "this crisis is a wake-up call."
A host of European refineries have shut down over the past years, with closures especially pronounced around the years 2010-2012 and the early 2020s. Refineries have closed across the EU, including in France, Italy, Portugal and Finland, as well as in non-EU countries like Norway and the UK.
The Commission said that "we need to counter this trend and ensure that we have adequate and flexible refining capacity in Europe."
It also noted that refining is strategic as "diesel and jet fuel are essential for mobility, the broader economy and defense" and hence it is necessary to create an environment that supports sufficient domestic fuel production in Europe.
It also urged refineries to "embark on a pathway of producing more bio and e-fuels" noting that renewables production has already compensated some of the reduced refining capacity and can "help us to further reduce risks from fuel imports."
Several refineries, mostly in Italy and France, have already converted or are currently converting to become biorefineries.
-- Elza Turner
Austria's OMV raises 2026 refining margin forecast by $5/b after strong Q3
Lifts 2026 margin outlook to $25/barrel
2026 refinery utilization expected over 90%
Company did not lift any UAE cargoes in Q3

Austria's OMV expects its European refining margin to average $25/barrel for the full year 2026 following an exceptionally strong Q3, up from the previous forecast of $20/b, the company said during its quarterly results presentation Oct. 29.
"I've been in this business 30 years and I've never seen refining margins as high as they were in Q3," CEO Emma Delaney said during a call with investors.
The Middle Eastern conflict, the Russia-Ukraine war, and very low middle distillate inventories contributed to the volatility of refining margins in Q3, according to Delaney, with the volatility expected to continue.
"When we look back to 2022-2023 and the spikes in refining margins at that time, it did take some time to unwind. And in Q4 in particular so far, what we are seeing at the moment, refining margins are bouncing around a lot," she said.
OMV, which operates refineries in Austria, Romania and Germany, expects its facilities' utilization rates to average above 90% for the full year 2026. No major maintenance is expected in the fourth quarter at any of OMV's sites, according to Delaney.
The company said it expects its hydrocarbon production this year to average around 290,000 barrels of oil equivalent per day.
OMV earlier this month reported that its output was 294,000 boe/d in Q3, up slightly from 291,000 boe/d in Q2 and 288,000 boe/d in Q1.
The company's CFO Reinhard Florey said on the call that OMV was unable to lift a single cargo from its UAE assets in the third quarter or in October.
OMV's Middle Eastern portfolio comprises upstream working interests of 20% in the UAE's SARB and Umm Lulu oil and gas fields and a 10% stake in Iraq's Khor Mor and Chemchemal gas fields. OMV also has stakes in ADNOC Refining, ADNOC Global Trading and Borouge petrochemicals businesses in the UAE.
"We will have to wait and see whether the lifting opportunities resume in the fourth quarter. The situation stays quite uncertain and the timing for the resumption cannot be provided at this point because so many open questions are still to be solved," he said.
"One thing is for sure, we will not lift at a loss, it is important that we are preserving our position. The good news actually is, we don't lose the barrels," Florey added.
The company expects Brent crude oil to average above $95/b for the full year, according to the latest OMV forecast. Dated Brent has averaged $96.94/b so far in 2026, according to Platts data. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
-- Alexandra Vladimirova

"I think the refining margin in Europe will remain quite good, quite strong during the first half of 2027, because we embark the consequences of prices, low inventories," Pouyanné said during a call with investors. "Beyond, it's very difficult to say what will happen," he said, adding that it is "one of the most complex questions" for the company of what view to take on refining margins for the next year.
TotalEnergies expects its refinery throughput in Q4 to stay roughly unchanged from the third quarter, with the 465,000 barrels/day Satorp in Saudi Arabia refinery returning to normal operations, despite a 20-day turnaround at its Normandy refining and petrochemical platform.
The company processed an average of 1.55 million b/d of crude across its refining network in Q3, a 9% increase quarter over quarter and 5% year over year.
"The increase of nearly 10% refining throughput enabled the company to fully capture historically high refining margins in Europe and worldwide, while oil trading activities outperformed at a level comparable with the two previous quarters," TotalEnergies said.
The quarter-over-quarter increase was primarily driven by higher refinery throughput in France, which rose to 426,000 b/d, up from 354,000 b/d in Q2, although French volumes were still lower than throughput of 481,000 b/d in the same quarter last year.
The main year-over-year increase was driven by a jump in refinery throughput in the rest of Europe, which rose to 688,000 b/d, up from 595,000 b/d in the third quarter of 2025 and 684,000 b/d in Q2 this year.
TotalEnergies said the quarter-over-quarter increase in refining production was driven by all refineries operating well in Europe and the US, as well as the progressive return of Satorp to its nominal capacity.
France's 220,000 b/d Donges refinery completed its major maintenance July 16.
TotalEnergies' global refinery utilization rate increased to 88% in Q3, up from 80% in Q2 and 84% in Q3 2025. In chemicals,

steam cracker utilization was 75%, up 4 percentage points from the previous quarter. In Europe, the company reached an average refining margin of $33.20/barrel, more than double the $13.50/b in the previous quarter and almost four times higher than in 2025.
TotalEnergies' sales of petroleum products were roughly unchanged quarter over quarter, at around 1.22 million b/d, but down from 1.27 million b/d in Q3 2025. The volumes excluded trading and bulk refining sales, the company clarified.
The decrease in product sales reflected "demand elasticity in a high price environment," TotalEnergies said.
The company said its downstream business reached "a record quarter," with adjusted net operating income of $4.1 billion, a 77% rise from Q2.
Despite very high profits, TotalEnergies is not considering expanding its refining capacities, Pouyanné said. The current situation rather encouraged it "to maybe consider maintaining these capacities."
"We will not change our strategy, you know, fundamentally, oil and gas upstream, its integration and maintaining," he said. "Downstream [for us] as a cash flow, and I think today's a super cash flow, so that's good for us."
Upstream production results Excluding the impact of the war, TotalEnergies expects its
hydrocarbon production to grow by more than 3% year over year in the fourth quarter, the company said in the quarterly statement.
"The impact of the conflict on production is currently decreasing compared to the third quarter and represents less than 5% of the company's total production, primarily related to limited exports of LNG volumes via the Strait of Hormuz," TotalEnergies said.
TotalEnergies produced 1.4 million barrels of oil equivalent/day of oil, including bitumen, in Q3 out of 2.47 million boe/d of overall hydrocarbon output. The results mark an 8% increase in oil production compared with the previous quarter. The company's overall liquids production averaged 1.5 million boe/d.
"A gradual return-to-normal oil production in the Middle East" drove the quarter-over-quarter increase in output, the company said.
Liquids production increased 19% quarter over quarter in the Middle East and North Africa to average 637,000 b/d in Q3. The produced volumes were still well below the regional output of 696,000 b/d in the third quarter of last year.
The Q3 decline in output year over year was 1%, according to TotalEnergies, as project startups and ramp-ups were unable to fully offset the decrease in production related to the conflict in the Middle East and the natural decline in fields.
The company attributed a 4% positive year-over-year production impact to growth mainly in two Brazilian fields -- Mero4 and Lapa Southwest -- as well as Anchor and Ballymore in the US, two Angolan projects, Mabruk in Libya and Ratawi in Iraq.

Neste sees lower refined products volumes, stable renewables in 2026
Q3 refining margin jumps to $48.2/barrel
Maintenance cuts refinery utilization to 51%
Renewable product sales hit record 1.29 mil mt
Finnish refiner Neste expects lower refinery production in 2026 -- following recent maintenance work -- as well as stable renewable fuel production, amid a volatile geopolitical landscape, the company said Oct. 29.
This comes after its oil product refining margin soared to $48.2/barrel in the third quarter, up from $15.5/b a year earlier, the company said in a statement accompanying its Q3 interim financial results. Key product margins were significantly higher than in the previous year, due to the ongoing conflict in the Middle East and refining capacity outages in Russia that are affecting global supply, Neste said.
"Going forward, we expect the markets to remain volatile," President and CEO Heikki Malinen said in a statement. Fuel markets have been very tight in recent months, boosting middle distillate prices in particular. "Energy security as well as availability and affordability of middle distillates are likely to stay high on the policy agenda around the world," Malinen said.
The Platts Forties Northwest Europe cracking netback margin averaged $39.08/b in Q3, up 222% on the year. The margin was $23.12/b on Oct. 28, compared with a five-year average of $13.29/b.

High freight costs have helped support crude prices and this has dampened refinery margins. However, the margins from refined products meant that this is not "a big concern," Neste CFO Eeva Sipilä said during an earnings call Oct. 29, presenting the company's results. Most of the company's crude comes from the North Sea, so its logistics chains are shorter than for some refiners, "and there is no geopolitical risk in that," Malinen said during the earnings call.
Neste's refinery output in Q3 was 1.725 million mt, down 41% on the year, due to maintenance at the company's 206,000 b/d Porvoo refinery, it said. The maintenance began in August and the company said Oct. 12 it had been completed.
Neste's refinery utilization rate in Q3 was 51%, down from 91% a year earlier, the company said.
Neste's total sales volume for its renewable products for the whole of 2025 was 4.13 million metric tons, while oil product sales were 11.87 million mt, according to its 2025 fullyear report.
The company also plans an eight-week maintenance period at its Rotterdam refinery and 11 weeks of maintenance on one of the production lines at its Singapore unit, starting in December, the company said.
Growing renewables In Q3, renewable product sales margin growth was supported
by stronger premiums across the core European and North American markets, as well as significantly higher fossil middle distillate prices, Neste said.
During the period, Neste's renewable sales volumes reached a record 1.29 million mt, up 23% on the year, the company said. Approximately 73% of the volumes were sold to the European market and 27% to North America, similar to a year earlier, Neste data showed. The proportion of waste and residue inputs was 92%, the company said.
In Europe, renewable diesel spot prices climbed above $3,000/mt in Q3, driven by global middle distillate market tightness, and sustainable aviation fuel prices at AmsterdamRotterdam-Antwerp traded at a discount to HVO due to robust import flows, Neste said. European feedstock prices firmed through the quarter due to improving regional demand, whereas Asian feedstocks remained comparatively soft, the company said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed SAF produced via the hydroprocessed esters and fatty acids pathway on a FOB basis at Flushing-Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp-Ghent at $2,865/mt through Q3, up 24% on the year. Platts assessed renewable diesel compliant with the EU's annex IX B renewable fuels on an equivalent basis at $2,908/mt through Q3, up 29% on the year, reaching a high of $3,166/mt on Sept. 15. These assessments were $2,497/mt and $2,928.25/mt, respectively, on Oct. 28.
"Our renewable fuels offer an alternative to fossil products and add to the supply of fuels, while reducing reliance on imports," Malinen said in the Oct. 29 statement.

Gradually rise Although the quotas were unlikely to be fully utilized, market
sources and analysts expected exports to gradually rise, with December volumes reaching 5 million-5.5 million mt amid increasing crude arrivals in November.
Most additional exports could materialize in December, potentially lifting outflows to a three-year high of 5 million mt, Energy Aspect said in a note dated Oct. 29. "We expect refiners to increase the gasoline-to-diesel export ratio, supported by recovering gasoline export margins and tight domestic diesel supplies. Jet fuel should continue to account for 55-60% of total exports."
The Asia/Singapore jet fuel crack averaged at $64.67/b as of Oct. 29, up from $55.82/b in September and $59.68/b in August, Platts data showed. The monthly average gasoil cracks in Asia/ Singapore remained hovering at high levels, although they retreated to $62.72/b as of Oct. 29 from $63.98/b in September and $67.72/b in August, according to Platts data.
Chinese refineries initially targeted to export up to 3.13 million mt of clean oil products in November, down 15% from an estimated 3.7 million mt in October, four sources close to the matter said Oct. 28.
Attracted by hefty profits, China's clean oil product exports hit 4.58 million mt in August, followed by 4.41 million mt in September, according to official customs data.

Market sources said quota availability was more than sufficient for the rest of the year, citing the 12.6% year-overyear export reductions in the first three quarters, lower refinery throughput expected in November and December, in addition to declining oil product inventories. Chinese refineries are required to prioritize domestic demand over export economics.
S&P Global Energy CERA analysts estimated that China would need to export at least 2 million barrels/day (around 7.72 million mt) of clean oil products over the final two months of the year to fully utilize the quotas. However, they added that oil product inventories were too low to support such export levels.

Japan's September naphtha cracker runs average at 70.2%, down from August
Ethylene output falls 5% to 371,400 mt
Negative margins pressure operations

The operating rates of Japan's naphtha-fed steam crackers averaged at 70.2% in September, down from 73.5% in August and 77.5% a year earlier, the latest data by the Japan Petrochemical Industry Association, or JPCA, said on Oct. 29.
Japan's ethylene production in September fell 5% from a month earlier to a four-month low of 371,400 mt, the data showed. The production also marked a 14% week-over-week decline, according to the data.
The operating rates of Japan's naphtha-fed steam crackers would remain low amid negative margins.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the CFR Northeast Asia ethylene price up $20/mt day over day to $1,230/mt on Oct. 29, while the CFR Japan naphtha price $3.25/mt was higher at $1,046/mt during the same period.
The price spread between ethylene and naphtha was calculated at $184/mt on Oct. 29, which can be compared with breakeven spread of $250/mt for integrated producers and $300$350/mt for non-integrated producers.

REFINERY NEWS: DGD targets stronger Q4 after hydrogen, logistics disruptions
Refinery: Diamond Green Diesel in Norco, Louisiana and Port Arthur, Texas
Owner: DGD (50% Darling Ingredients and 50% Valero Energy)
Overall capacity: 1.2 billion gallons/year
Units affected: Plants
Duration: Q3-Q4
Diamond Green Diesel expects to produce about 325 million gallons in the fourth quarter as it maximizes output amid attractive renewable fuel economics.
This follows a third quarter disrupted by a hydrogen supply outage, weather-related feedstock constraints and a delayed sustainable aviation fuel shipment, Darling Ingredients CEO Randall Stuewe said during the Oct. 29 earnings call.
During the company's third-quarter results call, the company said the fourth-quarter target output at DGD, a joint venture between Darling Ingredients and Valero Energy, is approximately 325 million gallons, with Darling providing renewable feedstock and Valero providing facilities and fuel marketing expertise.
Due in part to a short-term hydrogen outage in Norco, Louisiana, DGD's Q3 sales totaled 315 million gallons, below previous guidance.
While the company does not provide quarterly production numbers, two of DGD's three facilities are located on the site of Valero's St. Charles, Louisiana, refinery -- DGD 1 and DGD 2 --with a combined capacity of 675 million gallons/year.
"The hydrogen outage affected everyone in the Louisiana Gulf," said Stuewe on the call. "I think what's encouraging is it didn't affect our business for a very long time. Obviously, we guided -- I think it was 335 million gallons, and we came out with 315, so there was a difference in 20 million gallons."
DGD 3 is located at Valero's Port Arthur, Texas, refinery, which has renewable diesel capacity of 470 million gal/year, of which 50%, or 235 million gal/year, can be upgraded to sustainable aviation fuel.
"Port Arthur experienced challenging weather, forcing it to use a disproportionately high amount of foreign feedstocks, earning less production tax credit value than a normal feedstock mix," Darling's CFO Bob Day said on the call. "This had a double impact for Darling because while it lowered the average RD and SAF margin, it also caused DGD to hold a higher percentage of Darling

domestic feedstocks than normal." He also said a delayed SAF shipment added a logical
complication but did not provide further details. Separately, DGD filed with local regulators for planned work to
begin on Oct. 5 at DGD 3, but no specific details were mentioned on the call.
Darling's share of DGD adjusted EBITDA totaled $204 million, with an average margin of about $1.30/gal, which lagged replacement economics due to hedging timing, reduced production tax credit capture and the falling value of RINs credits, which Day noted fell from about $2.45/RIN to below $2/ RIN during the quarter.
In Q4, Day said said timing and hedging headwinds had reversed and realized margins were moving closer to stronger spot values, as higher diesel prices and attractive replacement margins were supporting output even with lower RIN values.
As of Oct. 29, D4 biodiesel RINs are averaging $1.98/RIN in Q4, down about 11% from Q3's average price of $2.23/RIN, according to Platts assessments. Platt is part of S&P Global Energy.
According to S&P Global Energy CERA biofuel analysts, renewable diesel demand and production are expected to rise under the high 2027 biofuel obligation. SAF should also grow, but its near-term expansion is more dependent on credits and project execution. For both fuels, the central question is whether producers can secure enough qualifying feedstock and sustain high operating rates, according to the Oct. 8 Biofuels Market Outlook.

REFINERY NEWS: PBF completes fall turnarounds, plans higher Q4 rates on deferred work
Refinery: 6 US refineries
Owner: PBF
Overall capacity: 1 million b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Q3 2026-2027
PBF Energy has completed its fall refinery turnarounds, clearing the way for strong fourth-quarter runs, but expects a heavier maintenance schedule in 2027 after deferring work at Chalmette and Toledo, CEO Matt Lucey said on the company's third-quarter earnings call.
The Martinez hydrocracker and Paulsboro crude unit turnarounds began in September and were completed in October, as planned and within budget. PBF expects fourthquarter throughput of 925,000-985,000 barrels/day, with market conditions encouraging maximum production through the remainder of 2026, compared with the third quarter's actual system throughput of 923,800 b/d.
"The story on products remains a supply story," Lucey said on the Oct. 29 call, noting that the biggest disruptions in the oil market have materialized in product availability, which PBF was looking to capitalize on.

"These disruptions have coincided with what was an already structurally tight refining market," Lucey said.
Refining head Mike Bukowski said on the call that repairs completed during unplanned downtime at Toledo in May helped enable that plant's deferral.
In the fourth quarter, PBF expects throughput at its two US Atlantic Coast refineries to range between 300,000 b/d and 320,000 b/d, up from 293,800 b/d in the third quarter, when lower rates reflected planned work on the Paulsboro crude unit.
At its Toledo, Ohio, refinery in the Midwest, PBF expects fourth-quarter throughput of between 155,000 b/d and 165,000 b/d, slightly above the third quarter's 156,400 b/d. Throughput at PBF's US Gulf Coast refinery in Chalmette, Louisiana, is expected to average between 180,000 b/d and 190,000 b/d in the fourth quarter, up from 180,500 b/d in the third quarter.
At Chalmette, the pretreater and reformer, which were taken offline in May, have returned to service. Separately, PBF completed the first phase of a jet fuel yield improvement project during the third quarter, upgrading high-sulfur diesel to produce about 10,000 b/d of jet fuel. The company plans to double that capability to 20,000 b/d in 2027.
Lucey projected capital investment for the Chalmette project at less than $20 million and cited a roughly 20-cent/gal Gulf Coast value uplift from high-sulfur diesel to jet fuel, stressing that these investments improve yields rather than expand crude processing capacity and that no large capacity expansions are planned.
In California, PBF is maximizing its use of locally produced crude while benefiting from a product market dependent on imports.
Lucey said the state imports about 250,000 b/d of gasoline, or about 30% of demand, describing West Coast consumption as broadly steady over the past year, despite price volatility.
During the third quarter, PBF's two California refineries operated at 293,100 b/d while the San Francisco-area Martinez hydrocracker underwent planned work. With that completed, PBF expects fourth-quarter US West Coast refinery throughput of 290,000-300,000 b/d, given the deferral of planned work at Torrance until 2027.
In California, crude production, rather than pipeline takeaway capacity, remains the immediate constraint on increasing local supply, Lucey said.
PBF still has available space on its M70 pipeline serving Torrance. A restart of the San Pablo Bay pipeline could provide an outlet for additional production to Martinez if southbound capacity fills, although Lucey said the new owner's intentions were unclear.
PBF also acquired two hydrogen plants serving Torrance from Air Products in September. Integrating their maintenance and operations with the refinery is intended to improve utilization and reliability, rather than deliver an immediate change in overall cash flow.
-- Janet Mcgurty

REFINERY NEWS: India's Barmer eyes full run in FY 2027-28, operates at 60%
Refinery: Barmer, Rajasthan, India
Owner: Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd. (HPCL)
Overall capacity: 9 million mt/y (180,000 b/d)
India's state-run Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd. aims to operate the Barmer refinery at full capacity in fiscal year 202728 beginning in April, as operations ramp up following its July startup, company officials said Oct. 29.
The refinery has been operating at a run rate of 60% since August, according to company officials, with runs expected to gather momentum in the second half of the current FY 2026-27.
The new refinery has been sourcing natural gas to operate its processing units, company officials said.
The refinery's primary unit, the crude distillation unit, has been running consistently since September, reflecting stable operations after the preliminary phase, according to company officials.
The polymer cracker at the refinery is in the commissioning phase and is expected to start up in about six months, company officials said.
The refinery expects to begin polymer production in FY 2027-28 following the commissioning of its dual-feed cracker unit, company officials said, adding that propylene production is expected to start earlier, in January-March.
The Barmer refinery is also expected to produce diesel and LPG in FY 2026-27, according to company officials.
The refinery would focus on processing high-sulfur crude, including supplies from Venezuela, although transporting the Latin American crude through pipeline networks in western India to the landlocked refinery could pose cost challenges, company officials said.
The Barmer refinery has a petroleum intensity index of 26% and a Nelson Complexity Index of 17, according to the oil ministry. The refinery is a joint venture, with HPCL holding a 74% equity stake and the Rajasthan government holding the remaining 26%.
The refinery has a processing capacity of 180,000 barrels/ day and a petrochemical capacity of 2.4 million metric tons/year, including the world's largest polypropylene and polyethylene swing units, according to the ministry.
The refinery has been configured to process 17% domestic crude, with the remainder to be imported, company officials said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta
REFINERY NEWS: Finland's Porvoo "operating well" after maintenance
Refinery: Porvoo, Finland
Owner: Neste
Overall capacity: 10.5 million mt/y (206,000 b/d)
Renewable refineries: Singapore, Rotterdam, Martinez
Owner: Neste
Overall capacity: 4.5 million mt/y
Finnish refiner Neste's Porvoo refinery has "successfully managed" its scheduled turnaround and is "operating well and

ready for efficient production until 2029," it said Oct. 29. Porvoo underwent eight weeks of maintenance starting in August.
It said earlier in October that Porvoo had completed the turnaround. Neste also said that refinery utilization at Porvoo in the third
quarter of 2026 averaged 51%, down from 91% in Q3 2025, "reflecting the executed eight-week turnaround at the refinery." Neste also said it mitigated the impact of the maintenance "through inventory management, building stocks ahead of the maintenance and selling from them during the shutdown, which allowed us to keep serving our customers and to benefit from the exceptionally strong market."
Over the first nine months of 2026, utilization was 90%, unchanged from the year-ago period.
Porvoo produced 1.725 million metric tons in Q3, down from 2.91 million mt in the year-ago quarter and 7.187 million mt in January-September, down from 8.423 million mt in the previous year period, Neste said in its Q3 report.
The refining margin was $48.2/barrel in Q3, up from $15.5/b in the year-ago quarter and $30.4/b in January-September, up from $11.8/b in the nine months of 2025.
"Key product margins continued on a significantly higher level compared to the previous year due to the continuing conflict in the Middle East and the refining capacity outages in Russia affecting global supply," it said.
In the renewables sector, its Rotterdam facility will undergo eight weeks of maintenance during the fourth quarter. At its Singapore facility, one of the production lines will undergo 11 weeks of maintenance work starting in December.
"These maintenance shutdowns include investments in reliability, designed to support sustainably higher operational availability in the coming years," Neste said.
Neste's renewable production capacity had an average utilization of 97% in Q3, up from 79% in the year-ago quarter. Over January-September utilization was 79%, unchanged year over year.
Neste also said the 2.7 million mt/year capacity expansion of the Rotterdam renewables fuel refinery continues to progress. "However, the mechanical construction work has faced challenges related to construction workforce productivity," Neste said, adding that the expansion start-up is expected by the end of 2027.
"Once the investment is complete, the Rotterdam refinery will be the world's largest renewable fuels production site, serving especially European needs for renewable diesel and sustainable aviation fuel," it said.
-- Elza Turner, Ernest Puey
