REFINERY NEWS: Repsol Cartagena topping unit to restart in Q4
Refinery: Puertollano, Tarragona, A Coruna, Bilbao, Cartagena; Spain
Owner: Repsol
Overall capacity: 896,000 b/d
The topping unit at the Cartagena, Spain, refinery, which faced a fire in January, is expected to restart during the fourth quarter of 2026, Repsol CEO Josu Jon Imaz said Oct. 29 during a conference call with analysts.
Repsol's crude throughput fell 4% year over year in Q3 to 9.2 million metric tons of oil equivalent, weighed down by the Cartagena outage, according to the company.
The refinery successfully concluded a catalyst change in its hydrocracker in recent days, Imaz said. The halt lasted 18 days and was pushed back from May.
In its other refineries, Puertollano will carry out a turnaround of its conversion units in Q4, including the vacuum unit, mild hydrocracker and fluid catalytic cracker, but not the coker, Imaz said, without adding a time frame for the work.
During Q3, the company's conversion rate was 97.6%, down from 101.2% in Q3 2025. The nine-month rate in 2026 was 91.0% compared with 92.8% in the year-ago period.
Further ahead, Repsol expects to carry out some general work on hydrodesulfurization and visbreaker units, Imaz said. This should include previously announced planned maintenance for the visbreaker at Tarragona, scheduled by the company for Q1 2027.
The main turnaround in 2027 will be at the conversion units in Cartagena in Q4 2027, Imaz said, without providing details.
In its petrochemical business, the company expects an expansion of its Sines, Portugal, site to be concluded in Q4, with derivative activity expected to start in November.
The operation of a new 15,000-metric-ton/year ultrahigh-molecular-weight polyethylene (UHMWPE) unit at Puertollano is expected to start before the end of this month, Imaz said.
The Tarragona petrochemicals site will carry out maintenance on its cracker in 2027, Imaz said, without adding details.
Depending on capital constraints, Repsol is also "analysing the potential acceleration of a retrofit of a unit at A Corunna to produce 1.2 million metric tons/year of biofuels, split 50-50 between SAF and HVO," Imaz said. This would enable Repsol to cover its market positions under European mandates for renewable fuels, he said.
-- Gianluca Baratti

REFINERY NEWS: TotalEnergies Q3 utilization reaches 88%
Refineries (with full or partial ownership): Feyzin, Gonfreville, Donges, Leuna, Antwerp, Zeeland, SATORP, Port Arthur, SIR, Daesan, Ras Laffan
Owner: TotalEnergies
Overall capacity: 2.6 mil b/d
France's TotalEnergies on Oct. 29 reported an 88% utilization rate at its group of refineries in the third quarter of 2026, up from 84% in the year-ago quarter and up from 80% in Q2.
Refinery throughput rose compared with the previous quarter, underpinned by well-functioning refineries in Europe and the US as well as "the progressive return of the SATORP refinery in Saudi Arabia to its nominal capacity," the company said in its Q3 report.
In the first nine months of the year, utilization was 86%, down from 87%.
TotalEnergies expects refinery utilization in the fourth quarter to be "broadly comparable to ... [that] of the third quarter, benefiting from the return-to-normal operations at the SATORP platform in Saudi Arabia, and despite a turnaround of the Normandy platform of 20 days." During a conference call, it added that the progressive return of the SATORP refinery during the third quarter to its normal capacity was "successfully reached at the end of the quarter."
It previously said that it expects the SATORP refinery to "return to its nominal capacity at the end of the third quarter of 2026."
Overall throughput in the third quarter was 1.555 million barrels/day, up from 1.478 million b/d in Q3 2025. It was 1.535 million b/d in January-September, largely unchanged from 1.538 million b/d in the year-ago period.
Throughput at TotalEnergies' French refineries in the third quarter was 426,000 b/d, down from 481,000 b/d in Q3 2025. It was 414,000 b/d in January-September, down from 460,000 b/d in the same period last year.
Steam cracker utilization in the third quarter was 75%, down from 84% in the year-ago quarter.
Monomer production in the third quarter totaled 1.165 million mt, down from 1.326 million mt in the year-ago quarter.
Polymer output in the third quarter fell to 1.152 million mt from 1.174 million mt in Q3 2025.
-- Elza Turner

REFINERY NEWS: India's Vizag upgrade to help process high-sulfur crude: officials
· Refinery: Vizag (Visakhapatnam), Andhra Pradesh, India
· Owner: Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd.
· Overall capacity: 300,000 b/d
India's Vizag refinery is in the process of stabilizing its 3.5 million metric tons/year residual upgrading facility by testing high-sulfur crude including Venezuelan supplies, company officials said Oct. 29.
The stabilisation process at RUF involves subjecting crude to high-pressure and high-temperature reactions to process some of the heaviest bottom-of-the-barrel material, breaking down

molecules to add more value to the processing mechanism, they said.
The RUF stabilisation process is expected to continue into the next fiscal year beginning in April, 2027-28, they said.
HPCL commissioned the RUF in January, they said. Currently, the gain in terms of gross refining margin accruing from RUF ranges from 7 cents/b to $1/b, they said. The upgrade program has been designed to improve the refinery's capability to process heavy residues into high-value products such as diesel, naphtha and LPG, they said. Initial expectation was that the refinery's RUF, which was the first unit in the world to use LC-Max technology, would enable the highest conversion of bottoms and significantly improve the GRMs at full capacity from 2026-27 (April-March), they said. The refinery faced challenges in adjusting to the technology as the RUF is the first unit put into practical application using the technology, they said. The refiner ran the RUF at 60%-70% of the capacity, and was able to understand the operational process associated with the licensed technology, they said. They said a higher volume of high-sulfur Venezuelan grades could be processed at Vizag once the RUF is technically operating in full swing. The fully operational residual facility is expected to ensure gains in terms of GRM improvement of at least $3/b over the next 3-4 years, they said. The debottlenecking program at the Vizag refinery is expected to raise the overall capacity to 18 million metric tons/year (360,000 b/d) by 2030, they said. Vizag's modernization project involves residual and secondary upgrades, promising better distillate yields, they said. Vizag processed 268,000 b/d in September, down 15% year over year, the oil ministry update said. Vizag processed 303,000 b/d in the April-September period or H1, up 1.3% year over year, it said. Vizag processed 320,000 b/d in 2025-26 (April-March), or FY26, up 4.6% year over year, it said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta

Platts Brazil domestic refined products publishing schedule for Nov. 2
Please note that S&P Global Energy's São Paulo office will be closed on Nov. 2 for the All Souls' Day (Finados) holiday in Brazil.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will not publish price assessments for Brazil domestic refined product markets, or Brazil diesel and gasoline market commentaries on this day.
Latin American Wire will be published without Brazil domestic prices on Nov. 2. The Platts Brazil Fuels Daily market report will publish on Nov. 2 with information from Oct. 30, and run again on Nov. 4 with information from Nov. 3.

Platts to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent (ADB) Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of each month, effective Dec. 1, 2026.
The change follows a review of historical data sets and will align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of the ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward assessment of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
Platts proposed this change in a subscriber note published Sept. 21, 2026.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under the following price database codes:

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Assessment Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo01 Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo02 Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo03 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo01 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo02 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo03 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo01 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo02 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo03

The assessments will reflect value at 16:30 London time. Heard bids/offers and trades will be reported in real time throughout the day on Platts PGA005, along with other relevant market information, and the information published will be used to inform the physical assessment.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time

(0830 GMT). The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the
two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Assessments Oman CFR North Asia Murban CFR North Asia Minas Singapore close Minas London close Cinta Singapore close Cinta London close Widuri Singapore close Widuri London close Minas FOB Indonesia $/MMBtu

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Units of measurements and descriptions updated for Platts FOLicht ethanol prices The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their Units of measurements and descriptions updated, as summarized below. Hectoliter Pure Alcohol (HPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One HLTPA equals 1 hectoliter (100 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard hectoliter (hL), which measures the total volume of any liquid, HLTPA refers only to the pure alcohol content, regardless of the concentration in the original beverage.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine

pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.

· Technical and non-technical professionals, with experience or new recruits, from crude oil and refined products companies
